Xiaomi has released MiMo v2.6, a family of open-weights language models that includes a Flash variant with 309B total/15B activated parameters and a Pro variant with 1.02T total/42B activated parameters. Alongside the weights, Xiaomi published a detailed technical report and a realtime reinforcement-learning training dashboard at mimo.xiaomi.com/rl/ that streams live training metrics. The release stands out for its unusual transparency: Xiaomi publicly streamed its reinforcement-learning post-training runs and shared comprehensive methodology, which is rare even among open-weights releases. It also adds to the growing momentum of competitive Chinese open models, with community members citing affordability as a key driver of interest. The MiMo-V2.6-Flash-RL and MiMo-V2.6-Pro-RL checkpoints are available on Hugging Face, and the live dashboard streams real-time steps, reward curves, token throughput, benchmark scores, and a cost counter for the two RL post-training runs. A fast-speed edition, MiMo-V2.6-Pro-UltraSpeed, is also offered through OpenRouter.

**Background**: Open-weights models are AI models whose trained parameters are freely downloadable, allowing researchers and developers to run, modify, and study them, though they may not include open training data or code. Mixture-of-experts architectures like MiMo v2.6 use a large total parameter count but activate only a small subset per token, which improves efficiency. Reinforcement learning post-training is the stage where a model's behavior is refined using reward signals after initial pretraining.

## [NASA and ESA Cancel Mars Sample Return Mission](https://www.science.org/content/article/nasa-s-mars-sample-return-mission-dead) ⭐️ 8.0/10

NASA and the European Space Agency have canceled the joint Mars Sample Return (MSR) mission, which was designed to retrieve rock and dust samples collected by NASA's Perseverance rover in Jezero crater. The decision follows years of severe cost overruns, with program costs reportedly rising to around $11 billion and a sample return date slipping to as late as 2040. The cancellation is a major setback for astrobiology, since returned Martian samples could be analyzed on Earth with far more sensitive instruments than any rover can carry, potentially revealing signs of past life. It also cedes near-term leadership in Mars sample return to China's Tianwen-3 mission, which is targeting launch around 2028 and sample return by roughly 2031. The NASA-ESA plan relied on the Perseverance rover to cache samples, an ESA-built Earth Return Orbiter to capture the sample container in Mars orbit, and a NASA-led lander and Mars ascent vehicle; earlier concepts also included an ESA Sample Fetch Rover. Even the revised architecture remained too expensive amid cost overruns across other NASA science missions.

hackernews · Muhammad523 · Sep 21, 19:14 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49791939)

**Background**: Mars Sample Return is a proposed multi-mission campaign to collect Martian rock and dust and bring it to Earth, where laboratories can study it in far greater detail than onboard rover instruments allow, especially for signs of ancient life. NASA's Perseverance rover has already been collecting and caching samples in Jezero crater as the first leg of the campaign, and the mission was formally approved in 2022 as a NASA-ESA collaboration. China's Tianwen-3 would be that country's first Mars sample-return effort, aiming to return at least 500 grams of material.

## [Bryan Cantrill's Retrospective on Sun Microsystems' Strategic Mistakes](https://bcantrill.dtrace.org/2026/09/20/what-sun-got-wrong/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Bryan Cantrill published a blog post titled "What Sun got wrong" analyzing the strategic mistakes that led to Sun Microsystems' decline, which sparked a 241-comment discussion on Hacker News with firsthand industry perspectives. The piece and its discussion offer a valuable post-mortem on how a once-dominant systems company lost its way, providing lessons for today's platform vendors about hardware lock-in, open-source strategy, and adapting to commodity x86 and Linux competition. Commenters highlighted specific missteps such as Sun briefly cancelling Solaris on x86 in 2002 and failing to strike a deal with Google in 2002 because Sun insisted on knowing how many servers Google had, while others noted the painful enterprise sales process compared with Dell's direct model.

hackernews · chmaynard · Sep 21, 14:03 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49787436)

**Background**: Sun Microsystems was founded in 1982 and became famous for selling high-performance Unix workstations and servers based on its own SPARC processors and Solaris operating system. Solaris originated influential technologies such as DTrace and ZFS, but the rise of inexpensive x86 servers running Linux eroded Sun's proprietary hardware and software business. Sun struggled through the 2000s and was acquired by Oracle in 2010, after which Solaris was renamed Oracle Solaris and eventually became closed-source again.

<a id="item-4"></a>
## [Malicious 'mathmain' npm Package Hides Encrypted Loader Triggered by a 3x3 Matrix](https://safedep.io/mathmain-encrypted-loader/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A security investigation by SafeDep reveals that the npm package 'mathmain', which presents itself as a simple math solver, contains an encrypted loader that only decrypts and executes its malicious payload when a specific 3x3 matrix is passed to its solving function. The package's GitHub repository and author account have since been taken down, but the package itself remains live on the npm registry without any warning. This incident highlights the growing sophistication of npm supply chain attacks, where malicious packages use conditional triggers to evade automated scanning and only activate in targeted environments. It underscores the risks developers face when installing seemingly benign dependencies, especially as high-profile npm attacks like Shai-Hulud have already compromised hundreds of packages. The trigger is a specific 3x3 matrix that unlocks the encrypted second stage, which then attempts to establish remote access; however, community analysis suggests the second stage is completely broken and non-functional. The package remains available on npm, while its GitHub repository and author account have been removed, raising questions about registry takedown processes.

hackernews · abhisek · Sep 21, 18:33 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49791378)

**Background**: npm is the default package manager for JavaScript and Node.js, hosting millions of open-source libraries that developers install as dependencies. Supply chain attacks occur when attackers publish malicious packages or compromise legitimate ones to inject code into downstream projects. CommonJS is an older module format used by Node.js that allows dynamic require() calls, which can make it harder to statically analyze dependencies for malicious behavior compared to modern ES modules (ESM).

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://safedep.io/mathmain-encrypted-loader/?ref=upstract.com">Why Does an npm Math Library Need an Encrypted Loader?</a></li>
<li><a href="https://grokipedia.com/page/Sha1-Hulud_npm_supply_chain_attack">Sha1-Hulud npm supply chain attack</a></li>
<li><a href="https://www.trellix.com/blogs/research/npm-account-hijacking-and-the-rise-of-supply-chain-attacks/">npm Account Hijacking and the Rise of Supply Chain Attacks</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

**Discussion**: Commenters expressed fascination with the intricate target selection, with one wondering why a specific 3x3 matrix would serve as a trigger. Others argued that the CommonJS module format should be abandoned because its dynamic require() calls are harder to audit than ESM's static imports. A commenter noted that the second stage is completely broken, and another raised concerns about whether law enforcement follows up on such backdoors and why the package remains on npm without warning.

**Tags**: `#security`, `#npm`, `#malware`, `#supply-chain`, `#javascript`

## [Cloudflare Python Workers reach general availability](https://blog.cloudflare.com/python-workers-ga/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Cloudflare announced that Python Workers are now generally available, following two years of development since the initial launch. The release highlights standardization through PEP 783 (Emscripten packaging) and upstream contributions to HTTP clients like urllib3 so they can route requests through the JavaScript fetch API in WebAssembly environments. General availability signals that Python on Cloudflare's serverless edge platform is production-ready, giving Python developers a first-class path to deploy at the edge without managing servers. It also strengthens the broader Pyodide/WebAssembly Python ecosystem by driving standardization and upstream library support. The standardization of PyEmscripten packaging via PEP 783 makes it easier to distribute binary Python packages for the Pyodide runtime, and upstream urllib3 contributions added Pyodide/Emscripten and JSPI support that enable Requests to work. Cold-start performance remains an open question raised by the community, since WebAssembly-based Workers historically had longer spin-up times.

hackernews · torutofu · Sep 21, 13:38 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49787142)

**Background**: Cloudflare Workers is a serverless platform that runs code at the edge across a global network. Python Workers run Python inside WebAssembly using Pyodide, a port of CPython to WebAssembly/Emscripten that lets Python packages run in browser and Node.js environments. PEP 783 defines a 'pyemscripten' platform tag so binary wheels can be built and distributed for the Pyodide runtime.

## [Fiber cut halts East Coast flights as FAA backup line also fails](https://www.reuters.com/world/us/faa-halts-some-us-east-coast-flights-due-communication-issues-2026-09-21/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

On September 21, 2026, construction contractors cut a Verizon fiber-optic cable serving the FAA's Philadelphia TRACON facility, prompting the agency to halt incoming flights at Newark Liberty, Philadelphia, Teterboro and other busy Northeast airports. When controllers tried to switch to the backup circuit, they discovered that the backup fiber was also broken and had not been monitored, leaving no redundancy at all. The outage grounded flights across one of the busiest air corridors in the United States, causing major economic disruption and raising serious questions about the resilience of life-critical aviation infrastructure. It shows that even a single physical cable cut can cascade into a national airspace crisis when backup systems are neither diverse nor actively monitored. The primary circuit at Philadelphia TRACON failed first, and the backup fiber was only discovered to be broken at the moment of failover, meaning it could have been unserviceable for days, weeks or months without anyone knowing. The FAA has been rolling out a new air traffic control system called Smart ATC, but this incident occurred on the existing legacy infrastructure.

hackernews · allanbreyes · Sep 21, 18:41 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49791509)

**Background**: TRACON stands for Terminal Radar Approach Control, a FAA facility that guides aircraft during the approach and departure phases within roughly a 30- to 50-mile radius of an airport. Air traffic control relies on fiber-optic links to carry radar, voice and flight-plan data between facilities, and standard practice is to have at least two physically separate paths so that one cut does not take the system down. A 'backup' that is not continuously tested or monitored provides no real protection, which is exactly the failure mode seen here.

Others shared war stories of fiber cuts caused by backhoes and even travelers cementing over an inspection cover, underscoring how common physical damage is.

A rare PDF of Tim Schafer's original 1996 puzzle design document for Grim Fandango was shared on Hacker News, drawing 348 points and 88 comments. The document, originally posted by Double Fine for the game's tenth anniversary, reveals Schafer's handwritten-style notes, jokes, and even a hidden trick where he faked the final puzzle description. This document offers a rare, unfiltered look at the creative process behind one of the most acclaimed adventure games ever made, serving as a valuable case study for game designers and software engineers. It also sparks discussion about how modern development culture might discourage such personality-filled internal documents. The document includes a playful note asking readers to 'restrict your fallen tears of joy' to a small box, and on the second-to-last page, Schafer admits he hadn't designed the ending puzzle yet, so he wrote two nonsense paragraphs and overlapped them to simulate a print formatting error. The PDF is hosted on gameshelf.jmac.org and was originally shared by Double Fine.

A puzzle design document is an internal planning artifact that outlines the game's puzzles, and this one is celebrated for its unusual personality and humor.

Higgsfield's platform aggregates multiple video generation models such as Kling 3.0, Veo 3.1, and Sora 2, and its flagship Cinema Studio feature offers professional cinematic video generation.

## [CogGym: A Scalable Framework for Comparing Human and Machine Cognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21259) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced CogGym, a scalable, unified framework grounded in cognitive science that systematically compares model and human behavior on matched experimental trials. Its initial release curates and standardizes 258 cognitive experiments from 100 papers focused on commonsense reasoning, and evaluates 50 large language models against human responses. This provides a rigorous benchmark and infrastructure for measuring where AI models resemble or diverge from human cognition, which could spur further research in both AI evaluation and cognitive science. It shifts comparison from ad hoc task sets toward reproducible, large-scale, living evaluation. CogGym uses a semi-automated, human-in-the-loop pipeline to standardize diverse experimental paradigms into a task-agnostic Experiment Markup Language (EML). The study finds a clear scaling trend where larger and more recent models better reproduce human judgments, but model-human fit remains well below human split-half reliability, with the best models reaching R² = 0.59 on text, 0.58 on image, and 0.43 on video experiments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Commonsense reasoning is the human-like ability to make presumptions about ordinary everyday situations, and it has long been a challenging area for AI. Cognitive science provides experimental paradigms that measure how humans actually respond, but comparing those results with model outputs at scale has been difficult because tasks and formats vary widely. CogGym addresses this by encoding experiments in a common markup language so that human and machine responses can be compared on the same trials.

## [LLM Tree-Expression Serialization Is Lossy and Asymmetric](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21509) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21509) proposes a round-trip protocol in which one language model converts a procedurally generated arithmetic expression into a word problem and a separate model recovers the expression, using symbolic equivalence as an exact oracle. Testing all pairwise combinations of sixteen models produced a communication matrix showing that swapping generator and extractor roles shifts accuracy by up to 60.4 points, with the best pair reaching 92.9%. The findings identify natural-language serialization of hierarchical structure as a primary bottleneck in multi-agent LLM systems and chain-of-thought reasoning, where models routinely exchange free-text intermediates. Because the channel is asymmetric, simply choosing which model generates versus extracts can matter more than which model is used at all, which has direct implications for how LLM communication protocols are designed. At least 73.6% of round-trip failures originate at generation rather than extraction, and difficulty is driven by tree structure—operator count, depth, and right-branching—rather than model family. The channel is also trainable: roughly 3,600 fine-tuning examples sharing the evaluation's operators and tree shapes lifted every open-weight model above untrained Gemini-3.1-Pro, and a disjoint-domain regime with new operators and vocabulary still improved every open-weight model, though a gap to the frontier remains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought prompting and multi-agent frameworks often have language models pass structured information—such as mathematical expressions or plans—through free-text messages, effectively serializing a tree into natural language and deserializing it on the other end. Serialization is a standard computing concept (as in Protocol Buffers or JSON) where a data structure is encoded into a transmittable format and later reconstructed; the paper asks how much information is lost when the format is ordinary prose. A round-trip protocol measures this by encoding and then decoding the same object and comparing the result against the original with an exact symbolic oracle.

## [TaxiGPT Study Shows Transformers Learn Faithful World Models Despite Failures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21748) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21748) presents a mechanistic analysis of TaxiGPT, a transformer trained on random walks through Manhattan, showing via causal interventions that it represents intersections and streets, tracks its position, and uses a goal compass to navigate. The authors trace its behavioral failures to interference between superposed intersection features that disrupts localization, and propose mechanistic indicators to compare models and track when world-modeling capacities emerge during training. This work challenges the common inference that behavioral failures imply a missing world model, suggesting instead that apparent failures can stem from representational interference. It motivates a shift from asking whether a model has a world model to mechanistically studying how it represents and uses its environment, with implications for transformer interpretability and AI cognition research. The analysis uses causal interventions to identify specific internal components, including intersection and street representations, position tracking, and a goal compass. Affordance packing, which groups intersections with the same legal moves, helps limit the consequences of localization errors, and the proposed mechanistic indicators reveal that world-modeling capacities emerge at different stages of training.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer the internal computations of neural networks, often focusing on transformers. A key concept is superposition, where a network represents more features than it has dimensions by assigning them to nearly-orthogonal directions, which can cause interference between features. TaxiGPT is a transformer trained on random walks through Manhattan whose failures were previously interpreted as evidence of an incoherent internal map.

## [PIR: A Lie Detector That Reads Hidden Knowledge in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21996) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces Probe of Internal Recognition (PIR), a reference-free method inspired by the forensic Concealed Information Test that detects whether a language model internally recognizes a correct answer even when it does not report it. Across eight models from five families (Gemma, Qwen, Llama, Mistral, and Phi), PIR recovers the recognized answer at 0.70–0.87 balanced accuracy, far above the 0.28–0.40 unknown-item baseline and 0.25 chance rate. The method addresses a core problem in AI safety and evaluation: distinguishing a model that will not answer from one that cannot, which is central to detecting sandbagging, deceptive alignment, and verifying unlearning. It could support capability audits and unlearning verification across the model ecosystem. PIR remains readable across every concealment form tested—prompted deception, trained sandbagging, and external password-locked and circuit-broken checkpoints—with recognition between 0.85 and 0.93, and the signal is causal and adds information beyond black-box behavioral cues. It also extends from multiple-choice questions to free-form generation, and recognition drops to the level of never-known questions when unlearning genuinely removes the knowledge.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: The Concealed Information Test (CIT), also called the Guilty Knowledge Test, is a forensic polygraph technique that presents a suspect with the true detail among plausible decoys and measures a stronger physiological response to the recognized item. Large language models can hold knowledge they do not report—for example, sandbagging on a capability evaluation or answering against what they internally know—and their outputs alone cannot reveal whether they are hiding an answer or simply lack it. PIR adapts the CIT idea to model internals: it presents a question with candidate answers and reads from the model's internal states which candidate the model recognizes as correct, without needing an honest reference model or a labeled truth corpus. Balanced accuracy is a classification metric that averages per-class recall, making it robust to class imbalance and thus suitable for comparing against a chance rate.

## [Designer-RSI evolves procedural memory for agentic graphic design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22086) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Designer-RSI introduces a continual adaptation framework where a frozen frontier model operates professional design software through over 230 tools while an external procedural memory of natural-language skills accumulates and refines reusable design procedures from real user traffic. Across five rounds on 1,406 real user briefs and 1,869 automatically graded trajectories, with no weight updates or human labels, the skill bank grew from 76 to 139 skills and raised GenEval2 execution success on Claude-Sonnet-4 from 72.7% to 99.3%. This work shows that agents can continually adapt to noisy, unverifiable real-world feedback without fine-tuning or human labels, which is significant for long-horizon creative tasks like graphic design where no reliable programmatic oracle exists. The approach could broadly impact AI agents and design automation by offering a practical route to skill accumulation under imperfect supervision. The memory widens by acquiring procedures for recurring uncovered subtasks and deepens by revising existing procedures against their own successful and failed executions, while a matched replay gate admits only changes that repair failures without regressing observed successes. On 200 held-out briefs, widening or deepening alone reached 49.4% and 48.6% win rates over the no-skill agent, while their combination reached 58.5% (p = 0.025).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: AI agent memory is an architectural capability that lets an agent retain and reuse information beyond the immediate model call, and procedural memory specifically stores reusable procedures or skills. Continual learning without weight updates avoids catastrophic forgetting and the engineering risks of updating model parameters in production, instead letting agents adapt through external memory. Designer-RSI applies these ideas to professional graphic design, a long-horizon task where structured, editable artifacts emerge from many interdependent actions and outcomes lack a reliable programmatic oracle.

## [BI-Agent and BI-Bench: Automating End-to-End Business Intelligence with LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20886) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.20886v1) introduces BI-Bench, the first benchmark for systematically evaluating LLMs on end-to-end business intelligence tasks, built from real-world BI projects and manually extracted question/ground-truth answer pairs. The authors also present BI-Agent, a tool-augmented agent that decomposes BI workflows into search, join, and transform subtasks and can be post-trained with SFT and RL, achieving accuracy gains of up to 40 percentage points over vanilla LLMs and up to 30 points after post-training. Business intelligence is a core enterprise workflow, and this work shows that even frontier LLMs score below 50% on realistic end-to-end BI questions, quantifying a major gap that affects tools like Power BI and Tableau. By combining tool-augmented reasoning with domain-specific post-training, the paper points to a practical path for automating data preparation and analysis, which could reshape how enterprises build and consume BI dashboards. BI-Bench is constructed by harvesting real-world BI projects from public sources and manually extracting question/answer pairs from actual user dashboards, and the paper reports that frontier LLMs achieve less than 50% accuracy on it. BI-Agent orchestrates specialized data management methods across BI stages, and the post-training framework synthesizes training trajectories from real BI projects for both SFT and RL.

## [Test-Time Communication Scales Multi-Agent Performance Superlinearly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21032) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21032) shows that a team of k communicating agents, called team@k, matches the success rate of 4k independent agents on the ARC-AGI-3 benchmark, with the advantage growing as k increases. The same effect transfers to research-oriented tasks: on polyomino packing, communicating agents exceed the prior best-known score, and on MNIST classifier compression, a team of four agents produced a 1,957-byte classifier with 99.4% test accuracy, surpassing the best-known human solution. This result challenges prior mixed findings on whether communicating agents help, showing that test-time communication can enable tasks no single agent can solve and that gains compound with scale. It has direct implications for how multi-agent AI systems are designed and scaled, suggesting communication should be treated as a first-class scaling axis alongside compute and model size. The gains are not unconditional: independent agents may outperform communication when compute is limited or when there is no clear measure of progress, but under sufficient compute and clear feedback, multi-agent communication consistently yields stronger results. Agents in the study have no predefined roles and communicate via a shared directory, making the mechanism simple and general.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: ARC-AGI-3 is an interactive reasoning benchmark from the ARC Prize Foundation that challenges AI agents to explore novel environments, acquire goals on the fly, build adaptable world models, and learn continuously; it is described as the world's only unbeaten benchmark for agentic intelligence. Multi-agent systems (MAS) consist of multiple AI agents interacting in a shared environment to solve problems that are complex for a single agent, and prior work has shown that such agents can develop emergent collaborative behaviors. This paper studies test-time communication, meaning communication that happens during inference rather than during training, as a way to scale performance.

## [Physically Based Rendering in the Latent Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21054) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21054) introduces physically based rendering (PBR) directly in the latent space of diffusion models, enabling light transport simulation within the feature space learned by variational autoencoders. The authors modify the rendering equation and pair it with a differentiable renderer to produce latent maps for physically guided content generation, training on a single rendered image and generalizing to changes in geometry, lighting, and camera view. This work bridges classical physically based rendering and latent diffusion models, potentially improving the controllability of generative AI by injecting physical light transport constraints into the generation process. It could benefit both computer graphics and generative modeling communities by enabling physically guided content generation with minimal refinement. The method modifies the rendering equation to operate in the latent space and uses a differentiable renderer to optimize scene parameters that require only minimal refinement to accurately render into a pretrained latent space. It is trained on a single rendered image and demonstrates generalization to scene geometry changes, lighting changes, and camera view changes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Physically based rendering (PBR) is a computer graphics approach that simulates light and surface interactions using optics to achieve photorealism, often via ray tracing or path tracing. Latent diffusion models are generative models that compress data through autoencoders and perform diffusion in a low-dimensional latent space, while variational autoencoders (VAEs) learn a smooth probabilistic latent space for compression and generation. This paper combines these ideas by performing PBR within the VAE latent space of a diffusion model.

## [SWE-Proof: Machine-Checked Proofs Replace Tests for LLM Coding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21190) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Benchproofer, a pipeline that converts real-world coding issues with known correct patches into formally verified tasks, producing SWE-Proof—a benchmark of 500 issues from SWE-bench Verified whose correctness is machine-checked rather than tested. It also extends to SWE-bench Pro, and across two frontier models verification caught counterexamples in a quarter to a half of test-passing patches. Held-out test suites are inherently incomplete and increasingly vulnerable to memorization, so replacing them with formal verification could substantially change how LLM-generated code correctness is evaluated and trusted in real software engineering. The finding that many test-passing patches are actually incorrect suggests current benchmark scores may overstate model coding ability. A correct formal specification lifts resolution from 85% to 95% for Opus 4.8, but writing that specification is the hard part: models that must write their own gain nothing over an unaided baseline, and only 62% of their specifications pass the audit, with the usual failure being unfaithful specifications that constrain only part of the required behavior. Specification quality still tracks outcomes, failing on 89% of unresolved instances versus 47% of resolved ones.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: SWE-bench is a widely used benchmark that asks language models to fix real GitHub issues in large repositories, and SWE-bench Verified is a human-filtered 500-instance subset used in official leaderboards; correctness there is judged by running held-out tests. Formal verification instead uses mathematical specifications and machine-checked proofs to guarantee that code meets its intended behavior, but prior work only handled standalone tasks with given specifications, not real repository-level issues stated in vague natural language.

## [EvoPilot: Human-Gated Agents for Long-Horizon Online Autoresearch](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21257) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21257) introduces EvoPilot, a human-gated method for long-horizon online autoresearch in which role-specific LLM agents execute each round through a versioned domain skill and typed adapter, with durable records and deterministic checks enforcing recorded lessons. In a 37-day campaign on the retrieval system behind Video Deep Dive (VDD), EvoPilot traced a spurious 22-percentage-point offline hit-rate drop to a pre-existing evaluation defect, then measured a 3.20-percentage-point offline improvement after repair, while a separate seven-day randomized online evaluation estimated a 0.66% relative gain in the VDD slice of Good Search Result Rate for Retention (GSRR). The paper argues that a completed autoresearch run can still support an invalid conclusion when code changes are no-ops, data windows leak, evaluator semantics drift, or the two experiment arms traverse different serving funnels — failure modes that matter as LLM agents move from minutes-scale self-contained loops to weeks-long campaigns that can influence a shipped product. Its verification-over-trust stance offers a concrete protocol for teams deploying agentic model development on real retrieval and recommendation systems. The campaign covered seven research directions over an hourly refreshed index of hundreds of millions of videos, and the human-gated verification revealed that the apparent 22-point drop came from an evaluation defect producing output depths of 3,000 and 600. Post-study replay and mutation tests rejected invalid comparisons while admitting valid counterparts, durable state recovered an interrupted round, and artifact reuse saved roughly five GPU-hours.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Autoresearch refers to LLM agents that autonomously propose, implement, and evaluate model changes; earlier demonstrations ran in minutes on self-contained programs, whereas online autoresearch spans asynchronous production systems, hours-long variant runs, and weeks-long campaigns. Video Deep Dive (VDD) is an online experience that recommends follow-on videos after a user opens a seed video, and its retrieval system serves as the testbed here. Good Search Result Rate for Retention (GSRR) is the online metric used to gauge whether search results retain users.

## [AI Mimics Cooperative Talk Without Real Social Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21401) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A large-scale arXiv study analyzed 15,881 human-ChatGPT and 10,784 human-human multi-turn dialogues using mixed-effects models to compare how morality, politeness, and alignment function in each setting. It found a consistent dissociation: AI reproduces the surface features of cooperative communication—moral output, warmth, and linguistic convergence—without the underlying social architecture that grounds mutual adaptation between humans. The finding that AI's cooperative mechanisms can run in reverse—hedging and softening that increase accommodation between humans reduce alignment when produced by AI—challenges how conversational systems are evaluated and trusted. It suggests that turn-level politeness metrics are insufficient for judging interaction-level success, with direct implications for AI alignment, HCI, and conversational AI design. Moral output appears preconfigured rather than negotiated, warmth is generated without face sensitivity, and linguistic convergence declines persistently across turns. Agency—giving users room to shape the exchange—is the most consistent predictor of alignment in both interaction types, while lower moral assertiveness in more recent models does not translate into better cooperation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Conversational AI systems based on large language models produce fluent, socially appropriate responses, but it remains unclear whether they genuinely participate in cooperative communication or merely simulate its surface forms. Cooperative dialogue in humans relies on mechanisms such as politeness, face sensitivity, and linguistic convergence, where speakers adapt to each other turn by turn. This study uses mixed-effects models—statistical models suited to repeated measurements within the same conversational units—to identify which features predict turn-to-turn alignment.

## [CIPL: A Channel-Aware Framework for Recoverable Privacy Leakage in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21686) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced CIPL (Channel Inversion for Privacy Leakage), a channel-aware evaluation framework that models privacy leakage in black-box LLM agents as a six-stage pipeline: sensitive source, selection, assembly, execution, observation, and extraction. Experiments across memory-based, retrieval-mediated, and tool-mediated targets, plus a BrowserUse live-agent case study, show that storage labels alone do not determine whether an attacker can actually recover sensitive information. Existing privacy evaluations focus on individual components like memory or retrieval, making it hard to distinguish internal exposure from information an external observer can actually recover. CIPL provides a shared protocol for comparing how internal sensitive dependence becomes externally recoverable leakage across heterogeneous agent pipelines, which matters for AI security researchers and practitioners deploying LLM agents. Memory targets form a near-saturated reference case, retrieval-mediated leakage is frequently partial, and tool-mediated and live-agent leakage varies strongly with observation surface, prompt-to-channel alignment, retrieval depth, and provider behavior. A stratified semantic audit further identifies attacker-useful disclosures that canonical exact matching misses.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents are systems that combine large language models with memory, retrieval, and tool-use pipelines to perform tasks autonomously. Privacy leakage in such agents is typically evaluated component by component, which obscures whether sensitive data stored internally can actually be extracted by an external attacker. CIPL reframes this as a channel inversion problem, treating the agent pipeline as a communication channel that an attacker tries to invert to recover sensitive inputs.

<ul>

## [Touvigation: Hands-Free Object Finding for Blind Users](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21828) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers present Touvigation, a hands-free object acquisition system that combines vision-language understanding with persistent local spatial modeling to give blind and low-vision users low-latency, body-relative guidance in unfamiliar indoor spaces. In a study with 12 blind and low-vision participants, Touvigation achieved 100% task success, compared with 58% for a multimodal LLM assistant and 85% for unassisted search, while also reducing completion time and cognitive workload. This work shows that persistent spatial grounding and adaptive embodied guidance can substantially outperform general-purpose multimodal LLM assistants for a real accessibility task, addressing known weaknesses such as latency, hallucinations, and guidance poorly aligned with physical action. It could inform the design of future assistive technologies for blind and low-vision users and advance research in human-computer interaction and embodied AI. The system was designed based on formative interviews with eight blind and low-vision participants and uses a multi-stage guidance framework that adapts spatial references as users move from orienting, to walking, to reaching and tactile verification. The evaluation compared Touvigation against a multimodal LLM assistant and unassisted search, with the LLM baseline showing notably lower success and higher workload.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are AI systems that jointly interpret images and text, extending text-only large language models and enabling applications such as image captioning and visual question answering. Blind and low-vision users often struggle to locate and physically acquire objects in unfamiliar indoor environments, and while VLM-based assistants can describe scenes semantically, they may suffer from latency, hallucinations, and guidance that is poorly aligned with embodied action. Persistent spatial modeling maintains a stable representation of the local environment over time, which helps ground guidance in the user's actual position and orientation.

## [NemotronLabs VoiceChat: Open Full-Duplex Speech-to-Speech Model with Tool Calling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21967) ⭐️ 8.0/10

NemotronLabs released VoiceChat, an open full-duplex speech-to-speech model that natively supports tool calling, combining a streaming speech encoder, a decoder-only language model, parallel output streams for agent text and structured function calls, an auxiliary RNN-T branch for incremental user transcription, and a streaming TTS decoder. On Full-Duplex-Bench 1.0 it achieves the lowest pause-handling takeover rates among evaluated open-weight systems, 100% takeover after user interruptions, and a 4.33/5 post-interruption response-quality score, while scoring 82.5% tool-selection F1 on Full-Duplex-Bench 3.0. This is a significant open-source release because it shows that full-duplex interaction, speech recognition and generation, general language ability, and external tool use can be integrated into a single open speech-to-speech model without sacrificing real-time conversational behavior. It could accelerate the development of real-time voice agents that can listen, reason, call tools, and speak simultaneously, an area previously dominated by closed or narrowly scoped systems. The model's unified streaming architecture preserves the temporal behavior needed for natural conversation, and it resumes its response after user backchannels in 93% of cases on Full-Duplex-Bench 1.5. However, argument accuracy and end-to-end tool execution remain areas for improvement, and it obtains a 55.1 normalized average on VoiceBench.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Full-duplex speech-to-speech models can listen and speak at the same time, enabling natural turn-taking and interruption handling, unlike traditional half-duplex systems that must wait for the user to finish. Full-Duplex-Bench is a benchmark designed to evaluate the turn-taking capabilities of such models under realistic, real-time conditions, including pause handling, interruption recovery, and backchannel resumption. RNN-T (Recurrent Neural Network Transducer) is a sequence-to-sequence architecture commonly used for streaming automatic speech recognition, and here it serves as an auxiliary branch for incremental user transcription.

<a id="item-26"></a>
## [Fact Grounded Attention Eliminates LLM Hallucination via Attention-Level Knowledge Injection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.25252) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2509.25252v3) introduces Fact Grounded Attention (FGA), an architectural modification that injects verifiable knowledge directly into the pre-softmax attention scores of a transformer. On 1,107 technical queries about smartphones, laptops, and electric vehicles, FGA raised accuracy from 6.3% with vanilla Llama 3.2 to 99.7%, and knowledge updates take under one second without retraining. Hallucination is one of the biggest barriers to deploying LLMs in factual domains, and FGA claims to eliminate it entirely for verifiable facts rather than merely reducing it. If validated, this could shift the field from probabilistic post-hoc patching (like RAG or parameter editing) toward deterministic, attention-level grounding with near-instant knowledge updates. FGA intervenes at the mathematical heart of the transformer—the pre-softmax attention scores—rather than patching outputs after generation or prepending retrieved text. The paper reports knowledge updates in under one second versus hours for parameter-editing approaches, but the evaluation is limited to 1,107 technical queries in three product domains and remains a non-peer-reviewed preprint.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models like Meta's Llama 3.2 generate text by predicting the next token probabilistically, which makes them prone to hallucination—confidently producing fabricated or factually incorrect content. In a transformer, attention scores are computed as scaled dot products between query and key vectors, then passed through softmax to produce attention weights; FGA modifies these pre-softmax scores to inject verifiable facts. Existing mitigation strategies such as retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) prepend retrieved text, while parameter-editing methods modify model weights, but both have limitations in speed or reliability.

## [BoostAPR Uses Dual Reward Models to Boost Automated Program Repair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09134) ⭐️ 8.0/10

BoostAPR introduces a three-stage reinforcement learning framework for automated program repair, combining supervised fine-tuning on execution-verified demonstrations, dual reward models (a sequence-level assessor and a line-level credit allocator), and PPO optimization. Trained on SWE-Gym, it achieves 40.7% on SWE-bench Verified (+22.9pp over the base model), 24.8% on Defects4J, 84.5% on HumanEval-Java, and 95.0% on QuixBugs. Automated program repair is a core challenge in software engineering, and BoostAPR's line-level credit assignment addresses the sparse, coarse rewards that have limited reinforcement learning for code. Its strong cross-language generalization (Python-to-Java transfer) and state-of-the-art open-source results could accelerate the adoption of RL-based repair in real-world development workflows. The line-level credit allocator redistributes rewards to critical edit regions at an intermediate granularity naturally suited to code changes, rather than relying on sequence-level rewards alone. The framework is trained on SWE-Gym and evaluated on four benchmarks, with the Python-to-Java transfer on Defects4J demonstrating cross-language generalization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Automated program repair aims to automatically generate correct patches that eliminate software bugs without causing regressions, and it has become increasingly relevant with the rise of LLM-based code generation. Reinforcement learning for repair is hindered by sparse execution feedback and coarse sequence-level rewards that obscure which edits actually fix bugs. SWE-bench is a benchmark using real-world GitHub issues to evaluate whether models can resolve actual software problems, while Defects4J, HumanEval-Java, and QuixBugs are widely used Java and Python repair benchmarks. PPO (Proximal Policy Optimization) is a policy-gradient RL algorithm that improves training stability by avoiding overly large policy updates.

## [Planetary Prediction Engine Automates Geospatial Modeling from Natural Language](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26088) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers have introduced the Planetary Prediction Engine (PPE), an autonomous AI system that executes the entire geospatial modeling workflow directly from natural-language queries, synthesizing multimodal datasets on the fly from Data Commons and Google Earth Engine and fusing them with geospatial foundation model embeddings (PDFM, AlphaEarth). Across diverse tasks, PPE consistently outperforms state-of-the-art or manually tuned expert baselines, including a mean R² improvement from 60.0% to 76.8% across 21 CDC health indicators and a Recall@10 of 83.3% for nowcasting the 2026 DRC Bundibugyo Ebola outbreak. By combining autonomous multimodal planetary data discovery with targeted model optimization, PPE lowers the technical barrier to planetary-scale analytics, enabling rapid, customized, expert-level deployment for challenges such as food security, disaster risk, and disease outbreaks. The involvement of Google researchers and integration with major platforms like Data Commons and Google Earth Engine suggests high potential impact on how geospatial science is conducted. PPE searches over task-tailored model architecture families with automated overfitting guards, and in data-scarce settings it integrates localized proxies to double baseline accuracy in Nigerian food security indicators (R² of 66.1% vs. 31.5%). For the 2026 DRC Bundibugyo Ebola outbreak, PPE identified 15 of 18 newly invaded health zones across five weekly forecasts, a +10.3 percentage-point improvement over the public state-of-the-art modeling (~73%).

## [ThoughtMed-1M dataset and FOLTMed model advance medical visual question answering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06914) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced ThoughtMed-1M, a long-form medical visual question answering (VQA) dataset containing over one million VQA pairs, built from de-identified medical images and expert commentaries shared through clinician-oriented online resources using an advanced LLM combined with clinician-in-the-loop verification. They also developed FOLTMed, a foundational LLM trained on this dataset, which achieved state-of-the-art performance across 42 medical VQA benchmarks with a macro accuracy of 85.4%. Medical multimodal reasoning has been bottlenecked by the scarcity of VQA datasets that capture clinical reasoning and explicit image-text alignment, so a dataset of this scale combined with clinician verification could meaningfully accelerate research on clinically grounded multimodal LLMs. FOLTMed's strong benchmark results and more clinically coherent responses suggest a scalable paradigm that could eventually support real clinical decision support and medical AI applications. FOLTMed outperformed state-of-the-art models by 3 to 5 percent on the ThoughtMed-1M test set across factuality and similarity metrics, and the dataset is designed to capture structured clinical reasoning rather than simple short answers. The pipeline relies on de-identified images and expert commentaries from clinician-oriented online resources, with clinician-in-the-loop verification to ensure rigor.

Large language models (LLMs) have shown strong general capabilities, but their medical use is limited by the lack of large, high-quality datasets that pair images with expert reasoning. Clinician-in-the-loop verification means human clinicians review and correct model-generated data, improving reliability. ThoughtMed-1M and FOLTMed aim to fill this gap by scaling up clinically grounded image-text data and training a dedicated foundational model on it.

## [In-context addition localized to three attention heads in Llama-3-8B](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05145) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2505.05145) introduces a method that localizes a transformer's in-context learning of addition to just a few attention heads, and shows the findings generalize to four additional arithmetic and semantic task families. In Llama-3-8B-Instruct, the mechanism reduces to three attention heads with six-dimensional subspaces, where four dimensions track the units digit via trigonometric functions with periods 2, 5, and 10, and two dimensions track magnitude using low-frequency components. This work offers a rare fine-grained mechanistic account of how few-shot in-context learning is implemented in a real large language model, which could inform interpretability research and future model design. The involvement of prominent authors such as Michael I. Jordan and Jacob Steinhardt adds credibility to these findings. The authors derive a mathematical identity relating the 'aggregator' and 'extractor' subspaces of attention heads, enabling them to track information flow from individual examples to a final aggregated concept. The analysis relies on dimensionality reduction and decomposition of head output spaces, and code is available at https://github.com/xyVickyHu/addition-subspaces.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer the internal computations of neural networks, especially transformers, whose multi-head attention mechanism lets different heads specialize in different relationships. In-context learning refers to a model's ability to perform a new task at inference time simply by conditioning on a few input-output examples, without any weight updates. This paper studies a controlled version of that phenomenon, where the hidden rule is to add a fixed integer k to the input, making it possible to pinpoint exactly which components implement the rule.

## [Audit of GPTKB v1.5 Reveals Gaps in LLM Factual Knowledge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.07024) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper presents a multi-dimensional audit of GPTKB v1.5, a knowledge base of 100 million facts recursively elicited from GPT-4.1. The analysis finds that the model's factual knowledge diverges significantly from established knowledge bases and that its accuracy is much lower than previous fact-completion benchmarks suggested. This work challenges the reliability of mainstream fact-completion benchmarks, which are prone to availability bias, and suggests that LLM factual knowledge is less trustworthy than commonly assumed. It highlights inconsistency, ambiguity, and hallucination as major issues, pointing to new research directions in neuro-symbolic AI for extracting, consolidating, and verifying LLM knowledge. The audit is based on 100 million facts (or beliefs) elicited from GPT-4.1, analyzed qualitatively and quantitatively rather than through sampled fact-completion tests. The paper reports that inconsistency, ambiguity, and hallucinations are major issues, though the abstract is truncated and does not provide detailed per-dimension metrics.

<a id="item-32"></a>
## [LLMs Are Shannon Compressors, Not Solomonoff Induction Estimators, Paper Argues](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.05280) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2601.05280v5) argues that cross-entropy, negative log-likelihood, and related next-token objectives cannot by themselves implement Solomonoff induction, because they optimize fit to a supplied conditional distribution rather than a program-weighted universal mixture. The authors contend that scaling compute within a fixed objective improves fit without changing the underlying inductive principle, and that reaching an AI Singularity requires symbolic model synthesis through neurosymbolic methods already being adopted by frontier developers. The paper offers a rigorous theoretical bridge between Algorithmic Information Theory, LLM training objectives, and the long-running AI Singularity debate, potentially reshaping how researchers think about scaling limits and the necessity of neurosymbolic architectures. If correct, it implies that pure statistical scaling alone cannot yield the self-improving positive-feedback loop required for a Singularity, affecting AI safety research, AGI roadmaps, and industrial investment in hybrid systems. The authors clarify that the data-processing inequality and Levin non-growth remain valid, but for finite learners and finite observers these theoretical boundaries have less relevance, producing a drift between possible approaches. They interpret resource-bounded estimators as finite tools for mechanism search that show divergence, not violation, of algorithmic information conservation laws, and note that frontier models like Fable and Astra already embrace symbolic computation and can no longer be considered purely statistical LLMs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Solomonoff induction is a mathematical theory of inductive inference in which the best model is the shortest algorithm that generates the observed data, naturally formalizing Occam's razor; it is incomputable but can be approximated from below with more compute. Shannon lossy compression, by contrast, characterizes the tradeoff between bit rate and distortion under a fixed distribution, without searching for programs. Neurosymbolic AI combines neural networks with symbolic reasoning and knowledge representation to improve reliability, reasoning, and generalization, and has been called the third wave of AI.

## [LLMs Compute Verbal Confidence Automatically and Cache It](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.17839) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2603.17839) investigates how LLMs internally generate verbal confidence, finding across Gemma 3 27B, Qwen 2.5 7B, and Magistral Small 24B that confidence is computed automatically during answer generation and cached at the first post-answer position, then retrieved for output rather than computed just-in-time. Using activation steering, patching, noising, swap experiments, and attention blocking, the authors show these cached representations explain substantial variance in verbal confidence beyond token log-probabilities. This work provides mechanistic evidence that verbal confidence reflects automatic, sophisticated self-evaluation rather than post-hoc reconstruction, which could reshape how researchers approach uncertainty quantification, calibration, and metacognition in LLMs. The findings are significant for AI safety and interpretability, though their immediate impact is currently limited to the research community. The study focuses on Gemma 3 27B across TriviaQA, BigMath, and MMLU, plus Qwen 2.5 7B and the reasoning model Magistral Small 24B; attention blocking pinpoints that confidence is gathered from answer tokens, cached at the first post-answer position, and then retrieved for verbalization. Linear probing and variance partitioning reveal that cached representations capture answer-quality information beyond simple token log-probabilities, suggesting verbal confidence is a richer evaluation than a fluency readout.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Verbal confidence is a widely used technique in which an LLM is prompted to state its confidence as a number or category, allowing uncertainty estimates to be extracted from black-box models. Token log-probabilities are another common uncertainty signal, computed from the model's probability distribution over output tokens. Mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer the internal computations of LLMs, and activation steering is an inference-time method that manipulates hidden activations with fixed steering vectors to induce or probe specific behaviors.

## [Cooperative-Override Circuit Suppresses Nash Play in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.27167) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2604.27167v3) reports that three larger instruction-tuned models — Llama-3-70B, Qwen2.5-32B, and Qwen2.5-72B — lock at full cooperation in the named Prisoner's Dilemma with zero variance, while Llama-3-8B plays near-Nash. Using logit-lens analysis, the authors identify a distributed cooperative override circuit that leans toward Nash through roughly three-quarters of network depth before a late-layer surge toward cooperation, and in the 8B model this override is a single causally controllable direction in the residual stream (Spearman rho = 1.000 when clamped). The findings suggest that cooperative behavior in LLMs is driven by a word-triggered lexical circuit rather than genuine game-theoretic competence, which has direct implications for AI alignment and for trusting LLM behavior in strategic or multi-agent settings. Because the circuit can be measured, bounded, and causally steered, it offers a concrete handle for controlling cooperation — and shows that a single Nash-playing agent can contagiously collapse cooperation in mixed-model populations. The circuit is lexical: it survives name removal and payoff rescaling but disengages when "Cooperate" and "Defect" are replaced with neutral labels, and across 48 payoff-random games with neutral surfaces no model locks cooperative or shows general equilibrium competence. The size of the final-layer correction, not the intermediate surge, rank-matches chain-of-thought behavior across scale and two architectures.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability is a subfield of explainable AI that reverse-engineers neural networks into concrete structures, algorithms, and circuits. The logit lens is a technique that applies the final unembedding matrix to intermediate hidden states to decode what a model is "thinking" before its last layer. The Prisoner's Dilemma is a canonical game-theory scenario where rational self-interested players reach a Nash equilibrium of mutual defection, making LLM cooperation a notable deviation. Residual-stream steering works by injecting a direction into a model's internal activations to causally shift its outputs.

<a id="item-35"></a>
## [LLMs Fail at Strategic Play Due to Broken Observation-Belief-Action Links](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00226) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2605.00226v2) uncovers two fundamental gaps in LLM decision-making under incomplete information: an observation-belief gap, where internal representations of latent game states are more accurate than verbal reports but brittle, and a belief-action gap, where converting beliefs into actions is weak. Experiments with Llama 3.1, Qwen3, and gpt-oss show that acting optimally on decoded beliefs would improve payoffs in about 95% of games. This analysis exposes systematic vulnerabilities in LLMs that could lead to poor outcomes in high-stakes strategic domains like negotiation and policymaking, urging caution before deployment without robust guardrails. It also provides a mechanistic framework for diagnosing and potentially fixing LLM failures in multi-agent and game-theoretic settings. Internal belief accuracy degrades with multi-hop reasoning, exhibits primacy and recency biases, and drifts away from Bayesian coherence over extended interactions, even within short prompt contexts. Neither implicit belief-conditioning nor explicit prompt-based belief-conditioning consistently yields higher game payoffs, pinpointing the belief-to-action conversion as the key bottleneck.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Incomplete-information games are strategic scenarios where players lack full knowledge of other players' types, payoffs, or past actions, common in economics and negotiation. Game theory typically models rational agents as maintaining Bayesian beliefs—probabilistic estimates updated coherently as new information arrives—and acting optimally on those beliefs. LLMs are increasingly used as agents in such settings, but their internal decision processes remain poorly understood, motivating this mechanistic analysis.

<details><summary>References</summary>

## [LLM Agent Classifies Genetic Disease Severity with 93.55% Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19569) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers developed an autonomous AI agent that combines ReAct reasoning with Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) to classify 10,211 Human Phenotype Ontology (HPO) terms by genetic disease severity, using ACMG-endorsed severity guidelines and ACOG quality-of-life criteria to retrieve PubMed literature and generate verifiable reasoning chains. The agent achieved 93.55% accuracy (MCC 0.9237) at the phenotype level, with 82.6% to 91.4% of claims supported by direct evidence or valid inferences, and showed 95.2% concordance with the Mackenzie's Mission gene list in external validation. Genetic disease severity classification is currently subjective and labor-intensive, creating bottlenecks in genomic screening where commercial panels vary widely in size and overlap. This system enables standardized panel design by providing reliable, automated, evidence-supported classification, which could improve consistency across clinical genetic testing and reduce the manual burden on expert curators. Gene-level severity was aggregated across 8,738 gene-phenotype pairs, identifying 3,283 autosomal recessive pairs with severe or profound presentations. The agent independently verifies its own claims, and the reported evidence-support range of 82.6% to 91.4% indicates that a meaningful fraction of conclusions still rely on inference rather than direct literature evidence.

**Background**: The Human Phenotype Ontology (HPO) is a standardized vocabulary of phenotypic abnormalities used to describe clinical features of human disease, such as atrial septal defect. ReAct is a framework that lets large language models interleave reasoning traces with actions, while Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) lets a model retrieve and incorporate information from external sources like PubMed before answering. ACMG and ACOG are professional bodies whose guidelines define how genetic variants and conditions are assessed clinically.

The discussion quickly expanded beyond the essay itself into personal stories about quitting social media, the Tetris effect, and the economics of the ad-driven web. The post and its discussion highlight a growing unease among technically literate users about attention economics — the business model in which free services are paid for with users' time and focus. This matters because the people building and consuming these systems are increasingly questioning whether the trade-off is worth it, which could influence product design and personal media habits. The essay is a personal reflection rather than a technical paper, and the Hacker News thread mixes historical observations (such as Mosaic's 1993 full-text history search versus today's bookmark systems) with practical experiments in intentional media use. Commenters also note the Tetris effect — the tendency to see everything through the lens of one's most recent intense activity — as a way to understand how platforms shape perception.

## [Heretic: An Open-Source Tool for Removing LLM Refusal Restrictions](https://heretic-project.org/) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Heretic is an open-source project that automatically removes refusal restrictions (censorship) from large language models, installable via pip and runnable with a single command such as 'llm heretic Qwen/Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507'. It was written from scratch and does not reuse code from prior abliteration projects, and it has sparked discussion on Hacker News about its utility for security research and the technical and legal challenges of abliterated models. Abliterated models give users and security researchers local, unrestricted access to model capabilities that commercial providers deliberately refuse, which is valuable for tasks like reverse engineering and device modification. At the same time, the project raises unresolved questions about model safety, provider terms of service, and the likelihood that such open-weight models will face regulatory scrutiny. Abliteration works by using ablation to modify internal functions and eliminate refusal behaviors, but as commenters note, if the training data itself was shaped around refusals, the model may simply lack the knowledge to answer even after the refusal path is removed. Heretic is distributed as a pip package and integrates with the 'llm' command-line tool, making it accessible to users without deep machine learning expertise.

hackernews · Bluestein · Sep 21, 04:35 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49783101)

**Background**: Large language models are typically fine-tuned with safety alignment that makes them refuse certain requests, and research has shown this refusal behavior is mediated by a single direction in the model's internal representation. Abliteration is the technique of using ablation to remove that refusal direction, producing 'uncensored' models that comply with any instruction. Heretic automates this process, and the resulting models are sometimes called 'abliterated' or 'heretic' models.

A new Hugging Face blog post proposes reframing block removal in large language model (LLM) pruning as an Ising optimization problem, applying physics-inspired methods to identify redundant transformer blocks. The approach treats each block's keep-or-remove decision as a binary spin variable and minimizes an energy function to find the optimal pruning configuration. LLM pruning is a key technique for reducing inference cost and memory footprint, and framing block selection as a combinatorial optimization problem could yield better pruning decisions than greedy or heuristic importance scoring. If the physics-inspired formulation scales, it may inspire further cross-disciplinary research and improve compression quality for practitioners deploying large models. The Ising formulation maps block removal to a binary spin system where the energy function encodes interactions between blocks, allowing solvers such as simulated annealing or Ising machines to search the combinatorial space. The blog emphasizes that block-level (depth) pruning removes entire transformer blocks at once, which is more hardware-friendly than unstructured weight pruning but requires careful selection to avoid large accuracy drops.

**Background**: The Ising model originated in statistical physics to describe magnetic systems of interacting spins, and minimizing its energy is a prototypical combinatorial optimization problem now used in fields like operations research and hardware Ising machines. LLM pruning aims to shrink models by removing redundant parameters, layers, or blocks; block-wise pruning methods such as LLM-Pruner estimate block importance and drop the least important ones. Physics-inspired machine learning combines physical modeling with ML techniques, and this work applies that lens to model compression.

## [RBS-Attention: Training-Free Sparse Prefill Speeds Up Long-Context LLM Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20971) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces RBS-Attention, a training-free sparse-prefill method that uses two complementary selection branches — a centroid base branch and a radius-based rescue branch — to mitigate the 'mean dilution' failure mode in block-sparse attention. On H100 GPUs it achieves 20.65x standalone prefill-attention speedup, 11.92x vLLM prefill-attention speedup, and 5.97x end-to-end time-to-first-token speedup at 128K context on Qwen3-30B-A3B-Instruct-2507-FP8. Prefill is the compute-heavy phase that dominates latency for long-context LLM inference, so a training-free method that cuts prefill cost by up to 20x without retraining could make long-context deployment substantially cheaper and more responsive. Because it preserves regular block-sparse FlashAttention execution and works on existing models like Qwen3, it is directly applicable to production serving stacks such as vLLM. The rescue branch uses the maximum key-block radius and its prompt-, layer-, and head-dependent distribution to flag blocks at risk of being underestimated, and the two branches are independently thresholded and combined to control rescue-block contribution. On dense Qwen3-32B, RBS-Attention reaches 88.65 overall RULER accuracy versus 89.52 for dense attention, with additional evaluation on LongBench-v2, InfiniteBench, and Video-MME, plus experiments on block size, thresholds, and memory behavior.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: LLM inference has two phases: a compute-heavy prefill phase that processes the entire prompt and builds the key-value (KV) cache, and a decode phase that generates output tokens one by one. For long prompts, dense self-attention in prefill becomes the bottleneck, so sparse attention methods skip less relevant blocks of the attention matrix to save computation. Block-sparse FlashAttention makes this practical by applying block-partitioned sparsity masks inside optimized GPU kernels, but selecting which blocks to keep is error-prone when a block's average relevance hides a single highly relevant token — the problem RBS-Attention calls mean dilution.

## [Attention-Aware Routing Couples MoE Routers with Attention Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20974) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Attention-Aware Routing (AAR), which augments a Mixture-of-Experts router with temporal and spectral features extracted from a sliding window of attention weights, while keeping the base transformer frozen and training only routing parameters. On OLMoE, AAR improves GSM8K by +3.37 percentage points over a routing-only SFT baseline. The work suggests that routing decisions and attention are not independent but form a coupled circuit, which could reshape how researchers design and fine-tune MoE language models. It also introduces layer-selective routing as a controlled probe for studying what information attention carries at different depths. AAR is strongly depth-sensitive: applying it indiscriminately across layers can degrade factual retrieval, while mathematical reasoning gains persist when it is introduced deeper in the network, exposing a retrieval-reasoning tension. It also reduces long diverging generation, shortening incorrect answers while leaving correct answer lengths unchanged.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) language models replace a single dense feed-forward network with many expert networks, and a router selects and weights only a few experts per token, keeping computation efficient. Traditionally the router relies only on the token's hidden state, using limited contextual information, while attention weights capture how tokens relate to one another across the sequence. GSM8K is a widely used benchmark of grade-school math word problems that tests multi-step reasoning in language models.

## [CaLR Combines Causal Structure and Diffusion for Robust Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20981) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces Causal Latent Revision (CaLR), a framework that reformulates reasoning as constrained latent optimization by adopting a causal topology matrix (CTM) from an expert model and using implicit differentiation to perform gradient-guided 'thought revision'. CaLR achieves state-of-the-art diffusion language model performance on complex benchmarks, surpassing strong autoregressive baselines and showing superior robustness on constrained tasks like Sudoku. This work bridges the gap between autoregressive models, which suffer from local greediness, and diffusion language models, which often lack strict causal structure, potentially enabling more reliable multi-step reasoning in AI systems. If validated, the approach could influence how future language models handle complex, constraint-heavy tasks such as mathematical problem solving and logical deduction. CaLR enforces logical consistency through dynamic self-correction of intermediate steps during parallel generation, using a causal topology matrix extracted from an expert model and implicit differentiation to compute gradients through the optimization process. The paper reports state-of-the-art results on Sudoku and other constrained benchmarks, though no code release or detailed ablation studies are mentioned in the abstract.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Autoregressive language models generate text token by token, which can lead to locally greedy decisions that harm long-term coherence. Diffusion language models instead generate text by iteratively denoising a corrupted sequence in parallel, but they often lack the strict causal ordering needed for logical reasoning. Implicit differentiation is a technique from optimization that allows gradients to be computed through the solution of an inner optimization problem without unrolling all steps, and a causal topology matrix encodes the causal dependencies among variables or reasoning steps.

## [Topological Signatures in Attention Graphs Detect LLM Hallucinations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21096) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21096) introduces a single-pass hallucination detection method that analyzes Forman-Ricci curvature in attention graphs to identify information bottlenecks associated with hallucinated responses. Evaluated across multiple LLMs and two established benchmarks, the approach consistently outperforms existing attention-based and multi-response baselines. This work offers a computationally efficient, single-pass alternative to multi-response hallucination detection, which is crucial for real-time AI safety and interpretability. It also provides mechanistic evidence that impaired context sharing among tokens—manifesting as over-reliance on self-attention or information over-squashing—is a topological signature of hallucination. The method captures both semi-local and global information-flow characteristics of attention heads, with hallucinated responses consistently showing over-reliance on self-attention, diffused context retrieval from earlier tokens, or information over-squashing, especially in the final transformer layer. It achieves competitive performance across diverse LLM architectures without requiring multiple response samples.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Hallucination in large language models refers to fluent but factually incorrect or unsupported outputs. Attention graphs represent the flow of information between tokens during generation, and topological data analysis—such as Forman-Ricci curvature—provides mathematical tools to quantify structural properties like bottlenecks or over-squashing in these graphs. Prior work like TOHA and CodeTD has used topological divergence on attention matrices to flag hallucinations, but this paper focuses on curvature-based signatures within a single forward pass.

## [Fine-Tuning Reshapes LLM Representations Independently of Causal Task Components](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21113) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21113) investigates how fine-tuning alters internal representations in large language models, including attention patterns and layer-wise activations, and compares these changes with task-relevant components identified by EAP (e.g., attention heads and logit-level activations). It finds that EAP-identified components are concentrated in specific layers, but their layer distribution is largely uncorrelated with the layers that undergo the most substantial representational change during fine-tuning. This finding challenges the common assumption that the layers whose representations change most during fine-tuning are the same layers that causally drive task performance, which could affect how researchers design parameter-efficient fine-tuning, model editing, and interpretability methods. It also suggests that cross-task component overlap does not guarantee positive transfer and can even cause performance degradation between tasks of different natures, such as classification versus generation. The paper reports that fine-tuning on one task can degrade performance on another when the two tasks exhibit a high degree of overlap in their EAP-identified components, and that overlap does not translate into cross-task performance transfer if the tasks are different in nature. These results are based on analysis of attention patterns and layer-wise activations, with EAP used to localize causally important attention heads and logit-level activations.
