## [Offline Multimodal LLMs Tested for Air Operations Decision Support](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21390) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21390) presents a modular retrieval-augmented architecture that runs offline multimodal large language models in isolated, no-internet environments to give air operations analysts traceable access to doctrinal knowledge from text and image manuals. A pilot study with four Brazilian Air Force image analysts found the system matched human performance on an electronic-target identification assessment (8/10) while completing it in 7.1 minutes versus a human average of 26.5 minutes. This work shows that offline multimodal LLMs can serve as practical decision-support tools in secure, disconnected military settings where external computing resources are unavailable, potentially reducing analyst workload and speeding up time-critical tasks. It also establishes a baseline and a future evaluation protocol for comparing manual and AI-assisted reconnaissance reporting workflows. The manual reconnaissance target report (REMIR) task was rated highly demanding, with mental demand at 6.0/7 and effort at 5.0/7, and the architecture supports both text and image input from technical manuals while keeping answers traceable to original sources. The study is explicitly a pilot with only four analysts, so the results are preliminary and the authors propose a larger protocol for systematic comparison.

Air operations analysts often work under strict security constraints and limited connectivity, needing to combine written doctrine with imagery without access to cloud services. Offline multimodal LLMs run entirely on local hardware and can process both text and images, making them suitable for such isolated environments.

## [RRDrive Adds Risk-Aware Occupancy to Register-Based End-to-End Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21486) ⭐️ 6.0/10

RRDrive introduces risk-aware occupancy as a dense, temporally aligned, and trajectory-queryable representation for end-to-end autonomous driving, built on a register-based baseline. It achieves a selected-trajectory PDMS of 0.951, a 1.5% relative improvement over the baseline's 0.937, and improves average candidate PDMS by 7.7%. The work targets a known weakness of sparse scene representations in multimodal trajectory prediction, where incomplete evidence leads to poor candidate generation and unreliable ranking. If validated, risk-aware occupancy could improve both the quality and the selection of driving trajectories in end-to-end systems, benefiting autonomous driving researchers and developers. On the register-based baseline, bad and poor candidates make up 19.74% of the candidate set, and the oracle-best candidate ranks only 33.9th on average. In challenging scenes, RRDrive improves candidate PDMS by 30.2% and raises the Spearman correlation among good candidates from 0.26 to 0.67, and the authors also develop an external RiskOcc predictor that estimates risk-aware occupancy directly from sensor inputs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: End-to-end autonomous driving must produce a single executable trajectory from uncertain observations, and multimodal trajectory prediction improves robustness by preserving multiple plausible driving behaviors before final selection. Register-based architectures, such as DrivoR, add learnable register tokens to each camera to compress thousands of ViT visual tokens into dozens of scene tokens. PDMS is a composite driving score that evaluates perception and planning quality, including collision avoidance and lane integrity. Occupancy representations describe which parts of the 3D space are occupied over time, and RRDrive makes them risk-aware and queryable by candidate trajectories.

## [Paper Proposes Learning-to-Optimize as Missing Layer in AI-Native Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21519) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21519v1) argues that Learning-to-Optimize (L2O) should be treated as the missing architectural layer between optimization and AI-native intelligence in future communication networks. It introduces a generic four-stage L2O workflow — optimization, knowledge generation, surrogate learning, and runtime inference — and illustrates it with an NR-V2X relay-selection problem where MILP-generated solutions train a Graph Neural Network to reproduce near-optimal decisions in real time. Current AI-native network architectures embed intelligence across network functions but offer little guidance on how optimization knowledge should be systematically generated, transferred, and exploited by AI models. Reframing optimization algorithms as offline knowledge generators for neural surrogates could give network operators a principled way to combine the rigor of classical optimization with the low-latency inference of deep learning. Unlike existing L2O approaches that primarily focus on algorithm acceleration, this framework positions L2O as an architectural abstraction applicable across heterogeneous communication and computing systems. The paper is a position/vision piece without demonstrated results or community discussion, so its claims remain conceptual rather than empirically validated.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: AI-native networks are an emerging vision in telecommunications where AI is embedded directly into network functions rather than bolted on as an external management tool, enabling autonomous resource management and zero-touch operation. Learning-to-Optimize (L2O) is a research area that uses machine learning to automate and accelerate solving complex optimization problems. A neural surrogate model is a deep learning model that approximates a computationally expensive process — here, the output of an optimization solver — so that predictions can be made quickly at runtime. NR-V2X refers to 5G New Radio vehicle-to-everything communications, and MILP (Mixed-Integer Linear Programming) is a classical optimization technique for problems with discrete decision variables.

## [DSRec: Dual-Interest Cross-SSM Model for Sequential Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21548) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose DSRec, a dual-interest cross-SSM model that disentangles long-term and short-term user interests for sequential product recommendation. It uses a full-sequence Mamba for long-term modeling and a time-modulated SSM for short-term intent, connected by a residual cross-fusion mechanism, and reportedly outperforms state-of-the-art methods on public benchmarks. Sequential recommendation is central to e-commerce and content platforms, and Transformer-based models remain costly for long user histories. By combining SSM efficiency with dual-interest disentanglement, DSRec could offer a more practical path to accurate, low-cost next-item prediction at scale. The model addresses item polysemy by encoding each item into long-term interest embeddings via historical aggregation and short-term embeddings modulated by inter-click time intervals. A residual cross-fusion mechanism exchanges contextual information between the two branches while preserving semantic independence, and experiments on public benchmarks show gains over state-of-the-art baselines.

**Background**: Sequential recommendation predicts the next item a user will interact with based on their behavior history, and Transformer architectures have driven recent progress but scale poorly with long sequences. State Space Models such as Mamba offer efficient long-range modeling by evolving a hidden state over time, making them attractive for recommendation. Item polysemy refers to the same item playing different semantic roles depending on user context, which single static item encodings fail to capture.

## [Centroid-Guided Contrastive Loss Boosts Fraudulent Job Posting Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21599) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21599) introduces Centroid-Guided Contrastive Loss (CGCL), a loss function that unifies classification with densely formulated clustering via a centroid-driven top-k push-and-pull mechanism. The authors report state-of-the-art performance on EMSCAD, a public benchmark for fraudulent job posting detection, and have released the code on GitHub. This work matters because it shows that jointly optimizing for decision boundaries and latent-space structure can improve both accuracy and representation quality in fraud detection, a domain where subtle textual cues distinguish fake from real postings. It could influence how researchers design loss functions for other imbalanced or fine-grained classification tasks in NLP. CGCL combines a classification objective with a clustering objective, using a top-k push-and-pull mechanism guided by class centroids to reshape the latent space. The method is evaluated on EMSCAD, a public benchmark dataset, and the code is available at https://github.com/ali-ahmed925/CGCL_code.

## [Beacon: A Course-Specific RAG System to Lower Academic Help-Seeking Barriers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21600) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21600) presents Beacon, a course-specific Retrieval-Augmented Generation system that grounds its answers in approved teaching materials to offer private, immediate, module-aligned academic support in higher-education computing courses. Using a design-based research approach, the team evaluated Beacon through mixed methods — questionnaires and semi-structured interviews with students and staff — and found students considered its responses more trustworthy and better aligned with module content than unrestricted generative AI tools. Help-seeking disparities are a key driver of unequal student success, and general-purpose AI tools often give answers that are inaccurate or misaligned with module expectations. A course-grounded system like Beacon suggests educational AI can occupy an intermediary space between independent study and formal support, broadening access to guidance without replacing lecturers. Beacon deliberately uses pseudocode and scaffolded explanations rather than direct solutions to encourage independent learning, and participants valued it as a first point of support before consulting lecturers or official resources. However, students remained cautious about trusting AI-generated responses without verification, and the abstract is truncated, so full evaluation results and limitations cannot yet be assessed.

In education, course-specific RAG systems retrieve answers from the actual assigned materials rather than synthesizing from broad training data, making them better suited to module-aligned tasks. ## [Cal-OPD Calibrates Teacher Self-Deviation in On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21619) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Calibrated On-Policy Distillation (Cal-OPD), which estimates the teacher's self-deviation region using positive and negative privileged interventions and removes it from the observed teacher-student discrepancy. Experiments on mathematical reasoning benchmarks show Cal-OPD consistently outperforms standard OPD and its variants across model scales while retaining only about 52-65% of the original discrepancy as the optimization signal. This work addresses a real limitation in on-policy distillation for reasoning models: the learning signal is contaminated by the teacher's own deviations, which the student then indiscriminately learns. By providing a cleaner signal, Cal-OPD could improve how reasoning models are trained and is relevant to researchers working on knowledge distillation and LLM post-training. The method relies on privileged interventions to define the teacher's self-deviation region, and the issue is especially pronounced in privileged OPD where privileged information causes larger teacher-side likelihood shifts. Cal-OPD discards roughly 35-48% of the original teacher-student discrepancy, suggesting that a substantial portion of the standard OPD signal is noise rather than useful capability-gap information.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy distillation (OPD) trains a student model on its own generated trajectories while a stronger teacher provides token-level feedback, combining the on-policy relevance of reinforcement learning with the dense reward signal of distillation. Privileged information distillation gives the teacher extra information at training time that the student will not have at deployment, which can improve learning but also amplifies teacher-side shifts. Cal-OPD builds on these ideas by treating part of the teacher-student discrepancy as the teacher's own self-deviation rather than a capability gap.

## [RAVEL: Retrieval-Aware RL for Interactive Person Re-Identification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21924) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced RAVEL, a retrieval-aware online reinforcement learning framework that optimizes question policies for interactive person re-identification, initializing from supervised question generation and using rank feedback from the full question-answer-retrieval loop. On the Interactive-PEDES benchmark, RAVEL delivers progressively stronger retrieval performance across five interaction rounds, outperforming supervised baselines. This work reframes interactive retrieval as a sequential information-acquisition problem, showing that question value should be judged by the response it elicits and its downstream effect on retrieval rather than by offline imitation. It could influence how conversational and interactive search systems decide what to ask next, particularly in domains where evidence is partial and user feedback is costly. The paper establishes that candidate discriminativeness and perceived usefulness provide only weak supervision for the question-selection objective, and RAVEL instead observes the current Top-4 candidates directly while optimizing the question policy with rank feedback. Analysis shows RAVEL reallocates the questioning budget toward localized open-ended attributes, yielding the largest gains on initially difficult queries.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Person re-identification (Re-ID) is a computer vision task that recognizes the same individual across different, non-overlapping camera views, typically without relying on facial recognition. Interactive Re-ID systems let a user answer questions to progressively narrow down candidates, and the Interactive-PEDES benchmark (introduced with the LLaVA-ReID work at ICML 2025) provides a standard testbed for this setting. RAVEL applies online reinforcement learning to this sequential decision problem, where an agent must choose which question will create the most useful evidence for the next retrieval update.

## [ECG Biometrics Tested Under Exercise Stress and Cross-Session Drift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21962) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper evaluates ECG biometrics under realistic conditions of exercise-induced stress and cross-session variability, using a Siamese ResNet with late multi-lead fusion trained on cardiopulmonary exercise test data. It reports an intra-session rest-to-peak equal error rate (EER) of 1.7% and a state-of-the-art 3.9% EER on the public CYBHi dataset. Most prior ECG biometric studies only used single-session resting data, so this work shows that an intrinsic cardiac signature can remain reliable despite physiological and temporal drift. That robustness matters for using ECG as an authentication factor and for protecting sensitive cardiac data, and it also suggests ECG discrimination can serve as a pretext task in self-supervised learning. The method uses a Siamese ResNet with a late multi-lead fusion strategy and an exercise-and-time-aware evaluation protocol, plus public benchmarks. The reported EERs are promising but still evaluated on specific datasets, so generalization to other populations, devices, and longer time spans remains to be tested.

## [SCR-MF: Hybrid scRecover and Random Forest Framework for scRNA-seq Imputation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.16923) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers present SCR-MF, a modular two-stage workflow that combines scRecover for principled dropout detection with missForest for non-parametric imputation of single-cell RNA sequencing data. Across public and simulated datasets, SCR-MF achieves robust and interpretable performance comparable to or exceeding existing imputation methods in most cases, while preserving biological fidelity and transparency. Dropout events are a pervasive problem in scRNA-seq that obscure biological signals, so improved imputation methods can directly enhance downstream analyses such as clustering, differential expression, and trajectory inference. SCR-MF's emphasis on interpretability and computational efficiency makes it a practical option for researchers working with mid-scale single-cell datasets. SCR-MF separates dropout detection from imputation, using scRecover to identify which zero counts are technical dropouts versus true biological zeros, and then applying missForest's iterated random forest approach to impute only the detected dropouts. Runtime analysis shows a competitive balance between accuracy and computational efficiency, though the method is positioned for mid-scale rather than very large datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq) measures gene expression at the level of individual cells, but the technology frequently fails to capture expressed genes, resulting in many false zero counts known as dropout events. scRecover is an R/Bioconductor package that detects and imputes dropout values while preserving real biological zeros, and missForest is a non-parametric imputation algorithm that uses iterated random forests to fill in missing values. SCR-MF combines these two established tools into a single two-stage pipeline.

## [LEON: Operator-Structured Latent Transitions for World Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27259) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.27259) introduces the Latent Evolution Operator Network (LEON), which replaces Transformer-based latent predictors in World Action Models with context-modulated operator-based propagation plus additive forcing, grounded in the controlled Koopman generator view of evolution. Across two different WAM formulations, LEON improved closed-loop performance and robustness, and remained effective even when the full transition module was replaced. The paper argues that how latent transitions are realized is a consequential architectural choice separate from how predictive representations are learned or how prediction is coupled to the policy, which could shift WAM design away from token-interaction-centric Transformers. If the gains generalize, robot-learning systems that rely on latent world models may become more robust and better suited to control-relevant temporal structure. LEON organizes context-dependent transition variation around a shared evolution-operator structure while keeping a complementary additive-forcing path, and controlled dynamical systems are used to verify this evolution-specific inductive bias and the complementary roles of propagation and forcing. The work is a niche, technically oriented contribution with no community discussion reported so far.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: World Action Models (WAMs) augment robot policies by predicting how task-relevant scene states evolve under interaction, and recent versions do this in latent representation spaces to avoid generating full appearance-level observations while preserving control-relevant information. Koopman operator theory provides a way to describe nonlinear dynamical systems through linear evolution in a lifted observable space, and its controlled variant decomposes the generator into autonomous and control-dependent parts. LEON applies this controlled Koopman generator view to latent transitions, which are otherwise typically handled by Transformer predictors whose inductive bias centers on token interaction rather than temporal evolution.

## [Reinforcement Learning Controls Post-Coronagraphic Wavefronts in Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20880) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.20880) presents a fully data-driven reinforcement learning method for post-coronagraphic aberration correction in a simulated high-contrast imaging testbed. The agent controls a deformable mirror using focal-plane images plus physics-informed wavefront sensing information, successfully creating dark holes and approaching the performance of conventional wavefront control methods. This work suggests that model-free reinforcement learning could offer an adaptive, data-driven alternative to traditional focal-plane wavefront control, which is critical for direct imaging of exoplanets where starlight must be suppressed to subnanometric precision. If validated on real hardware, such methods could simplify or improve the extreme adaptive optics systems used in future exoplanet-imaging telescopes. The method is validated only on simplified simulations of a high-contrast imaging testbed, not on real optical hardware, and it approaches but does not exceed the performance of conventional wavefront control techniques. The study also evaluates different observation representations and control strategies, highlighting the importance of how focal-plane and physics-informed sensing data are encoded for the RL agent.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Direct imaging of exoplanets is extremely difficult because a star can be billions of times brighter than an orbiting planet. A coronagraph blocks most starlight, but optical aberrations cause residual starlight to leak through as speckles that hide the planet. Wavefront control uses a deformable mirror to correct these aberrations with subnanometric precision, and reinforcement learning is being explored as a way to learn such correction policies directly from data rather than relying on hand-designed controllers.

## [Bio-MF: One-Step EEG-to-fNIRS Generation for Hybrid BCIs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20904) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose Bio-MF, a latent-free one-step MeanFlow framework that generates fNIRS signals conditioned on EEG for hybrid motor-imagery brain-computer interfaces. It performs direct signal-space x-prediction, converts the output into MeanFlow velocity supervision, and completes inference in a single network evaluation, achieving 7.0 ms per fNIRS trial on an RTX PRO 6000 GPU — an 857x speedup over the 1000-step SCDM baseline. Hybrid MI-BCIs that fuse EEG and fNIRS can outperform EEG-only systems, but paired acquisition is often unavailable and existing cross-modal generation methods are too slow for real-time use. Bio-MF's one-step generation could make EEG-to-fNIRS synthesis practical for real-time BCI decoding, potentially broadening access to hybrid BCIs without requiring simultaneous fNIRS recording hardware. Bio-MF integrates Spatial-Temporal Interactive 4D Encoding, cross-modal classifier-free guidance, and noise-level-gated FFT regularization to preserve task-relevant hemodynamic structure under heterogeneous sensor layouts. On Dataset 1, EEG plus synthetic fNIRS improves accuracy over EEG-only by 3.37 and 4.15 percentage points for HbR and HbO respectively, with gains of 2.98 and 2.50 percentage points on Dataset 2 under an unseen 64-channel EEG montage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Motor-imagery brain-computer interfaces let users control devices by imagining movements, typically decoding EEG signals. fNIRS measures hemodynamic (blood-oxygen) changes and offers better spatial resolution and motion tolerance than EEG, so combining the two modalities can improve decoding. However, recording EEG and fNIRS simultaneously is cumbersome, motivating cross-modal generation methods that synthesize fNIRS from EEG alone; prior approaches like SCDM rely on slow iterative diffusion sampling.

## [Study: Advice Style Shapes How People Judge AI Financial Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20989) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A randomized vignette experiment with 285 U.S. adults across eight financial decisions independently varied three advice styles — AI, expert, and online community — while holding the underlying recommendation constant and displaying source labels. Advice style most strongly shaped message and safety appraisals, expert labels selectively raised perceived source knowledge, and appraisal models explained 69.2% of overall quality, 75.9% of trust, and 82.9% of intended reliance. As generative AI becomes a mainstream source of personal financial guidance, this study shows that people's judgments are driven as much by who appears to be speaking as by what is said, which matters for regulators, fintech designers, and anyone building advisory AI. It also suggests design goals should target grounded evaluation rather than simply maximizing user trust. The experiment used a vignette design in which source labels were shown while the recommendation itself stayed identical, and expert-style advice remained most preferred even when labels were removed. The high explanatory power of the appraisal models (69–83% of variance) indicates appraisals are strong predictors of downstream trust and intended reliance, though the sample of 285 U.S. adults limits generalization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: A randomized vignette experiment presents participants with short, standardized scenarios (vignettes) in which one factor, such as the stated source of advice, is randomly varied so researchers can isolate its causal effect on judgments. In human-AI interaction research, "appropriate reliance" refers to users accepting AI advice when it is correct and rejecting it when it is wrong, rather than blindly trusting or dismissing it. Generative AI tools are now widely used for personal finance questions, making it important to understand how people evaluate such advice.

## [PlantShade: Diffusion Model Predicts Plant Shadows for Agricultural Robots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21059) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces PlantShade, a dataset covering four plant species (soybean, tomato, sugarbeet, and strawberry) with top-down viewpoints and a supplementary light moving along a circular trajectory to cast dynamic shadows across multiple growth stages. The authors also propose a diffusion-based generative shade simulation model with temporal conditioning that can generate realistic shadows for unseen plants and support downstream robotic tasks such as perception, lighting control, and view planning. Shading is a critical factor influencing plant growth, yet simulating it realistically has been difficult, so a generative model that predicts plant shadows could improve lighting-aware decision-making in agricultural robotics and controlled-environment agriculture. The released dataset and model may also serve as a foundation for broader plant-aware modeling and AI-for-agriculture research. The dataset includes top-down viewpoints with a supplementary light on a circular trajectory, capturing dynamic shadows across multiple growth stages and diverse observation complexities, while the diffusion model uses temporal conditioning to simulate shade at different time stages. The paper reports both quantitative and qualitative evaluations, though the approach is validated only on the four included species and the abstract does not specify real-time performance or generalization limits.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models are a class of generative models that learn to reverse a process of adding noise to data, allowing them to generate new images by iteratively denoising random noise; they are widely used in computer vision, including image and video generation. In agriculture, light availability strongly affects plant growth, and plants respond to shade from neighbors, so accurately modeling plant shadows matters for tasks like optimizing supplemental lighting or planning robot viewpoints. PlantShade applies diffusion-based generation to this specific problem, combining a purpose-built shadow dataset with a temporally conditioned model.

## [FOCAL-VLA Adds Subtask-Guided Geometry Distillation to Robot Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21228) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21228) introduces FOCAL-VLA, a framework that combines subtask-guided geometry distillation with implicit world modeling for Vision-Language-Action models. It transfers geometric knowledge from VGGT into the VLA model by aligning geometry latents with features from subtask-relevant image regions, and adds implicit world modeling using Track4World features from current and future demonstration frames, without running either teacher model at inference time. Precise and long-horizon robotic manipulation remains a weak point for VLA models that map 2D observations directly to actions, and prior geometry or future-prediction supervision often drowns the model in redundant scene information. By focusing geometric learning on the current subtask and capturing the future 3D evolution of the interaction, FOCAL-VLA could improve real-world manipulation performance while keeping inference cost unchanged. The two complementary representations — subtask-focused geometry latents and Track4World-based future dynamics — jointly guide action generation, and the paper reports gains over baselines on both simulation benchmarks and real-world manipulation tasks. Because VGGT and Track4World are only used during training as teachers, the distilled VLA model incurs no extra inference overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action models are multimodal foundation models that take an image or video of the robot's surroundings plus a text instruction and directly output low-level robot actions; the concept was pioneered by Google DeepMind's RT-2 in 2023 and later popularized by open models such as OpenVLA. Geometry distillation is a teacher-student technique that transfers 3D structural knowledge into a model that only sees 2D input, while implicit world models encode environmental dynamics directly in learned latent representations rather than predicting future states explicitly. FOCAL-VLA sits at the intersection of these ideas, using VGGT for geometry and Track4World for future-frame dynamics as training-time teachers.

## [Task-Aware GRPO Framework for Cross-Domain PCBA Visual Question Answering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21276) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes a multimodal reasoning framework that unifies standards-derived, real-world, and auxiliary PCB-domain data into a single instruction format with verified reasoning traces, and introduces Task-Aware Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) that replaces exact-match supervision with multi-component semantic rewards for choice questions, distance-aware rewards for counting questions, and an auxiliary format reward. The system achieves an Overall Score of 83.24 on the official PCBA Standard-to-Real Grand Challenge leaderboard. This work shows that task-aware reinforcement learning rewards can bridge the domain shift between standards-derived samples and real production-line imagery in industrial inspection, a setting where exact-match supervision is poorly suited to heterogeneous output spaces. It offers a practical recipe for deploying vision-language models in PCBA quality control, where automated optical inspection is a critical but error-prone step. The framework combines answer-option semantic consistency correction, self-consistency voting, and multi-model arbitration at inference time to improve robustness, and it explicitly handles both choice-based and numerical counting tasks within one unified instruction format. The reported 83.24 overall score comes from the official challenge leaderboard, though the paper does not detail per-task breakdowns or failure modes in the abstract.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Printed Circuit Board Assembly (PCBA) inspection relies on automated optical inspection (AOI) to detect soldering errors, missing components, and misalignments during manufacturing. Vision-language models (VLMs) can reason over images and text, but they suffer from domain shift when trained on standards-derived samples and deployed on real production-line imagery. GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning algorithm popularized by DeepSeek that improves reasoning in large models by comparing groups of sampled outputs rather than relying on a separate value network.

A new arXiv paper (2609.21349) proposes a Situation–Internal state–Behavior Persona method that incorporates situation-dependent behavioral strategies into LLM-based role-playing of real individuals. The authors also introduce a new evaluation protocol with reference material for LLM evaluators and a newly constructed social media reply dataset, reporting that their method outperforms state-of-the-art in-context learning baselines. Faithfully impersonating real people with LLMs is hard, and this work suggests that modeling how a person behaves in different situations—not just what they remember—measurably improves fidelity. If the approach generalizes, it could benefit applications such as social media simulation, digital avatars, and personalized agents, and it also offers a more reliable way to evaluate role-playing of lesser-known individuals. The evaluation protocol achieves only moderate correlation with human judgment, and the approach was additionally tested on fictional-character benchmarks to show it extends beyond social media. The paper is a cross-listing on arXiv and, as an incremental contribution, does not claim a fundamental breakthrough in LLM role-playing.

This works reasonably for famous figures with abundant public text, but it struggles to capture how a specific person would react in novel situations, and LLM-based evaluation is unreliable for obscure individuals who lack widely known references. This paper addresses both problems by adding explicit behavioral strategies tied to situations and by giving evaluators grounded reference material.

## [KG-Chronos-2 Augments Frozen Time-Series Model with Knowledge Graphs for HEC-RAS Flood Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21381) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21381) introduces KG-Chronos-2, which couples a frozen Chronos-2 time-series foundation model with hydraulic project knowledge via exact-state residual decoding, graph-conditioned historical retrieval, and input-aligned correction. On a fixed benchmark of 64 24-hour windows across 4,675 cross sections in 71 reaches, it achieves event-balanced RMSE of 0.246970, reducing error by 14.13% versus frozen Chronos-2, 29.38% versus a hydraulic DCRNN-style model, and 39.54% versus recurrent GeoFNO. This work shows that a general-purpose time-series foundation model can be meaningfully improved for a specialized scientific domain by injecting structured project knowledge, rather than retraining the model. If such surrogate forecasting proves reliable, it could offer much faster HEC-RAS water-surface elevation predictions for flood risk assessment and emergency response, where full hydraulic simulations are computationally expensive. The method keeps Chronos-2 frozen and adds three components: exact-state residual decoding, graph-conditioned historical retrieval, and input-aligned correction; task-specific fitting uses the 2008 simulation, while evaluation covers the 2011 and 2002 simulations on a shared geometry. The 95% hierarchical-bootstrap interval for its RMSE difference from frozen Chronos-2 is [-0.075177, -0.016317], and it also achieves the lowest active-window and final-lead RMSE among the six completed systems.

Researchers introduced G-RELIC (Graph Based Contextual Relexicalization with Improved Consistency), a method that combines LLMs with a graph-based mapping mechanism to achieve one-to-one correspondence between original and surrogate clinical entities. It also adds a deterministic temporal repositioning algorithm, yielding a 30.4 percentage point gain in relational integrity (62.1% to 92.5%) and a 45.9 percentage point gain in temporal coherence (46% to 91.9%) without hurting privacy benchmarks. Relexicalized clinical datasets are widely used to share data while masking sensitive information, but inconsistent entity replacement across longitudinal records undermines their scientific value. By preserving structural, relational, and temporal coherence, G-RELIC could make synthetic clinical datasets far more useful for downstream analysis such as temporal modeling and cohort studies, while keeping re-identification risk low. The approach optimizes a graph-based mapping for one-to-one correspondence between original and surrogate entities, and uses a deterministic temporal repositioning algorithm rather than independent entity replacement. Evaluations were run on diverse real-world clinical datasets, and the reported gains come without compromising recognized privacy benchmarks.

Traditional methods replace entities independently, which can break relationships between entities and distort the timeline across a patient's longitudinal records. G-RELIC addresses this by using a graph to enforce consistent one-to-one mappings and by repositioning temporal expressions deterministically.

## [LoG-VGGT Enables Memory-Efficient Long-Sequence 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21437) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose LoG-VGGT, a memory-efficient framework for long-sequence 3D reconstruction that replaces full global attention with cross-window attention applied at only a small subset of transformer blocks, plus a global camera consistency refinement module where camera tokens interact with compact register tokens via cross-attention. Experiments show improved depth accuracy and robust camera pose estimation across multiple long-sequence benchmarks while maintaining competitive streaming reconstruction performance. Long-sequence 3D reconstruction is bottlenecked by the quadratic memory cost of global attention, so this work offers a practical path to scale transformer-based reconstruction to hundreds of frames without exhausting GPU memory. It matters for applications such as autonomous driving, robotics, and large-scale scene capture, where long video sequences must be reconstructed with stable camera poses. The cross-window attention is applied only at a small subset of transformer blocks, keeping memory bounded while propagating information across adjacent temporal windows, and the refinement module uses compact register tokens to enforce scene-level constraints over the whole sequence. The paper reports gains on long-sequence benchmarks and competitive streaming performance, though it remains an incremental architectural contribution rather than a fundamentally new paradigm.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: VGGT (Visual Geometry Grounded Transformer) is a feed-forward neural network that directly infers camera parameters, point maps, depth maps, and 3D point tracks from one or many views, offering a deep-learning alternative to traditional structure-from-motion pipelines. Transformer attention scales quadratically with sequence length, which makes full global attention over long image sequences prohibitively memory-intensive. Cross-window attention, inspired by window-based designs like the Swin Transformer, restricts attention to local windows while allowing information to flow between them, and register tokens are compact learned vectors used to aggregate global scene information.

## [2nd Place HANDS 2026 Solution Uses Single-Shot Trajectory Warping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21511) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A team won 2nd place in the HANDS 2026 Workshop Challenge (Dexterous Grasp Motion track, held with ECCV 2026) by editing a single GraspM3 demonstration instead of generating grasp motions step by step. Their policy observes the object once and outputs a 12-D warp of the demonstration, which is replayed open-loop, and the warp policy is trained with one-step PPO over all 4,824 training objects in parallel. This result shows that single-shot trajectory editing can outperform step-by-step policy generation for dexterous grasping, achieving the highest easy-track success rate of 94.61% among all submissions. It suggests a practical direction for robot learning where demonstration reuse and one-step reinforcement learning reduce error accumulation and training cost. The task involves the 12-DoF LinkerHand O6 hand producing reach-and-lift trajectories for unseen objects from randomized initial poses in simulation, where a per-step policy would make about 70 twelve-dimensional decisions. The method scored 94.61% on the easy track and 57.18% on the hard track of the private test set, with the gap indicating that generalization to harder objects and dynamics remains difficult.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Dexterous grasp motion generation asks a multi-fingered robot hand to plan a physically plausible sequence of joint movements that reaches and lifts an object. The HANDS 2026 workshop challenge, associated with ECCV 2026, uses the 12-DoF LinkerHand O6 and trajectories retargeted from the GraspM3 dataset, with evaluation in Isaac Gym. PPO (Proximal Policy Optimization) is a popular reinforcement learning algorithm, and one-step PPO treats each episode as a single decision, which suits the single-shot warping formulation.

A new arXiv paper introduces a dataset and benchmark for evaluating large language models on Chinese competitive debate adjudication, built from 182 organized matches judged by 120 professional judges using a shared rubric. After filtering incomplete records, the dataset contains 148 matches, 2,698 stages, and 20,542 exchange units with manually verified transcripts, and defines three tasks: winner-tendency prediction, stage-score prediction, and best-debater prediction. Existing debate datasets rarely pair fine-grained transcripts with professional judgments collected during real competitions under a shared rubric, so this benchmark fills a gap for studying how well LLMs understand interactive argumentation. It offers a testbed for measuring model agreement with expert human judges, which matters for any application where AI is used to evaluate or assist in structured human argumentation. Zero-shot evaluation of multiple LLMs achieved at most 66.2% winner-prediction accuracy, a highest Pearson correlation of only 0.250 between model stage scores and mean human ratings, and a highest best-debater prediction accuracy of 56.8%. The dataset preserves original stage scores, match votes, best-debater ballots, and adjudication rationales, with each match independently judged by three judges.

**Background**: Competitive debate adjudication requires judges to track how arguments develop through interaction and to score them under a shared rubric, which is a complex reasoning task. Large language models are increasingly evaluated on benchmarks that test their capabilities across diverse natural language tasks, but fine-grained, professionally annotated debate data has been scarce. This paper addresses that gap by collecting real competition transcripts and expert judgments for Chinese-language debate.

## [Pipeline Links 4,397 Papers to Code in Wikidata](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21667) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper presents an end-to-end reconciliation pipeline that harvests and validates 4,397 ⟨DOI, repository-URL⟩ pairs from software-centric journals JOSS, SoftwareX, and IPOL, plus SIGMOD ARI reproducibility reports, and models them in Wikidata using two application profiles aligned with schema.org and CodeMeta. Only 82 of the harvested repositories were already in Wikidata; human-reviewed batches have since created 4,182 new software items cross-linked to their articles. This work turns Wikidata into a connector between the scholarly record and archived source code, helping close the gap that leaves software artefacts isolated from the Linked Open Data cloud. It could improve semantic discovery, citation, and reproducibility for researchers, journals, and open-science infrastructure. The pipeline uses two distinct Wikidata application profiles — one for scholarly articles and one for software instances — enabling rule-based reconciliation at two granularities: lightweight inline publication references or standalone first-class Wikidata software nodes equipped with SWHIDs, Software Heritage's content-addressed identifiers. ## [BAPA: Balanced Prompt Adaptation Prevents Entropy Collapse in Test-Time Binary Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21743) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21743) theoretically analyzes entropy-induced collapse in imbalanced binary segmentation test-time adaptation (TTA) and proposes Balanced-Anchor Prompt Adaptation (BAPA), which combines Class-Balanced Anchors (CBA) and Dynamic Prompt Adaptation (DPA). Experiments across four domains show BAPA achieves the highest mean Dice among evaluated methods. Entropy minimization is a widely used TTA objective, but its failure in dense prediction tasks like binary segmentation has been poorly understood; this work identifies the collapse mechanism and offers a practical fix. The approach could improve robustness of segmentation models deployed on unlabeled, domain-shifted data without retraining. The collapse is established in a shared-shift model where the larger predicted class dominates updates, pulling minority predictions toward it and producing degenerate masks as entropy gradients vanish. BAPA freezes vision-language encoders and optimizes only text-side prompt residuals, with CBA giving foreground and background equal total loss weight and DPA refreshing anchors after each prediction update.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time adaptation (TTA) lets a pretrained model adjust to a new domain using only unlabeled test data, often by minimizing prediction entropy to encourage confident outputs. In binary segmentation, the model outputs a dense mask with thousands of pixel predictions, so class imbalance can let one class dominate the entropy-based update. Prompt learning adapts vision-language models by tuning small text prompts instead of the full model, which is efficient and preserves pretrained representations.

A new arXiv paper (arXiv:2609.21829v1) introduces FedDCN, a generalization of Deep Clustering Networks to the federated learning setting that simultaneously optimizes a reconstruction loss and a clustering loss. To handle non-IID data across clients, FedDCN generates synthetic data augmentations and adds a geometric regularization term for latent space alignment, with experiments showing effectiveness under both IID and non-IID assumptions. Federated learning usually assumes clients hold private, decentralized data, but real-world client data is often non-IID, which degrades clustering quality; FedDCN targets this gap in an under-explored area where federated deep clustering methods remain scarce. If it holds up, the approach could support privacy-preserving unsupervised analytics in domains such as healthcare, finance, and IoT, where data cannot be centralized. The method combines a reconstruction loss with a clustering loss and uses synthetic data augmentations plus a geometric regularization to align latent spaces across clients under non-IID distributions. The abstract is truncated and no community discussion or engagement signals are available, so the full experimental scope and comparisons remain difficult to assess.

Evaluated across multiple social interaction scenarios by three independent LLM judges, the fine-tuned models were not better at role-playing different personalities than their respective baselines, though fine-tuning improved the linguistic diversity of the Qwen models. This negative result challenges the common assumption that personality-aware fine-tuning is the best route to more consistent and controllable social agents, suggesting that data quality and domain alignment may matter more than the tuning method itself. It is directly relevant to researchers building LLM-driven social simulations, interactive agents, and robots, and to anyone working on LLM alignment and persona consistency. The study used three independent LLM judges to assess personality fidelity and provide evidence-based behavioral interpretations, but low inter-rater agreement limits confidence in the results. Generated text quality was mostly comparable between fine-tuned and baseline models, and the authors conclude that baseline models offer the best overall performance while future work should emphasize training data quality and domain alignment.

A new arXiv paper (arXiv:2609.21879) introduces a benchmarking framework that treats explanation quality as a core evaluation axis for VLM-based face recognition, proposing two criteria — relevance (reliance on identity-stable facial features) and faithfulness (alignment with visible image content without hallucinated features). The authors develop a methodology that constrains model outputs to a structured explanation format enabling automated querying and auditing, then benchmark several families of open-weight VLMs while jointly measuring face verification accuracy and explanation quality. Forensic face comparison requires decisions that are transparent and auditable, yet existing evaluations of VLMs for this use case focus almost entirely on recognition accuracy while the validity of generated explanations remains unquantified. By making explanation quality a first-class metric, this work could push the field toward more trustworthy, explainable face recognition systems and provide a foundation for future fine-tuning. The framework constrains model outputs to a structured explanation format so that relevance and faithfulness can be automatically queried and audited, and the authors release an open-source evaluation harness. Results highlight remaining shortcomings in the explanations produced by current open-weight VLMs, underscoring that accuracy alone gives an incomplete picture of model performance.

## [Forced-Structure Reduction Narrows Conway's 99-Graph Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11211) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper reduces Conway's 99-graph problem to a constrained 12-regular graph on 84 vertices by fixing one vertex and exploiting the λ=1 and μ=2 conditions, which force the neighborhood to be a perfect matching. The authors validate the reduction with CP-SAT by recovering the unique srg(9,4,1,2), prove that no circulant graph on Z/99 satisfies more than 68.0% of the CAISc constraints, and use an evolutionary program-search system to find a degree-preserving 4-vertex-switch tabu search whose best verified artifact scores 70.73%. Conway's 99-graph problem is a famous open problem in combinatorics with a $1000 prize, and this work reduces the exact search space while improving the best verified partial construction, potentially guiding future computational or theoretical attacks. It also demonstrates how modern constraint programming and evolutionary program search can be applied to hard combinatorial existence problems. The reduction encodes the problem as a CP-SAT model and validates it by recovering the unique srg(9,4,1,2); exhaustive enumeration shows no circulant graph on Z/99 exceeds 68.0% of CAISc constraints, and the evolutionary search produced a move that differs from the authors' own searches and crosses a plateau stable under them. The results do not resolve the existence problem, but they reduce the exact search space and improve the best verified partial construction found in the experiments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Conway's 99-graph problem asks whether there exists a strongly regular graph with parameters srg(99,14,1,2), meaning 99 vertices where every two adjacent vertices have exactly one common neighbor and every two non-adjacent vertices have exactly two common neighbors. Equivalently, every edge lies in a unique triangle and every non-adjacent pair is the diagonal of a unique 4-cycle. John Horton Conway offered a $1000 prize for its solution, and the problem remains open; a valid graph would have 693 edges. CP-SAT is Google OR-Tools' constraint programming solver that combines constraint programming with SAT techniques.

## [Balance of Benchmarks: Reweighting Benchmark Scores for Fairer Model Comparison](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30044) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced Balance of Benchmarks (BoB), a framework that reweights publicly reported benchmark scores using semantic density weighting, score equating, and task-relevant residual pooling. Evaluated on 605 configurations across 14 Artificial Analysis benchmarks and on WildScores (148 developer-reported benchmarks), BoB-Support raised family-mean Spearman correlation from 0.764 to 0.823 and cut MAE from 6.19 to 5.10 normalized points. Model comparison increasingly relies on aggregating many public benchmark scores, but uniform averaging over-weights densely benchmarked capabilities while curated suites discard useful evidence. BoB offers a principled, testable way to use existing benchmark evidence more fully, which could affect how leaderboards, model cards, and evaluation suites are constructed. BoB-Support also improved the three-model shortlist hit rate from 65.3% to 72.6% and reduced retrospective shortlist regret from 2.08 to 1.67 normalized points, while BoB-Constant reached a Spearman correlation of 0.831 and a hit rate of 74.6%. Density weighting additionally reduced average ranking changes when benchmarks were repeated, including as paraphrased copies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Public leaderboards often aggregate many benchmark scores into a single ranking, but different benchmarks cover different capabilities at different densities, so simple averaging can distort comparisons. Score equating is a standard psychometric technique for placing scores from different tests onto a common scale, and semantic density weighting adjusts how much influence each benchmark receives based on how much unique information it contributes. BoB combines these ideas with task-relevant residual pooling to produce task-conditioned model comparisons from public aggregate scores alone.

## [Reinforcement Learning Under Non-Markovian External Influence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.16056) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper studies reinforcement learning in Markov decision processes with continuous state and action spaces whose transition dynamics are perturbed by an external, non-Markovian process. It establishes tractability conditions based on a finite history of exogenous events, proposes a policy iteration algorithm with policy improvement guarantees and sample complexity bounds, and analyzes the case of discrete-time Hawkes processes with Gaussian marks. This work extends reinforcement learning theory to settings where external events break the Markov assumption, a common real-world scenario in finance, epidemiology, and networked systems. The theoretical guarantees and sample complexity results could guide the design of robust RL algorithms for environments with exogenous shocks. The algorithm learns policies contingent on the current state and a finite history of prior exogenous events, but it is not guaranteed to converge; instead, policy improvement is guaranteed only in regions determined by approximation error. The sample complexity analysis accounts for approximations from truncating the event history, and the results apply to general discrete-time processes with a certain rate of decay of event influence, with a specific analysis for Hawkes processes with Gaussian marks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning typically assumes that the environment follows a Markov decision process (MDP), where the next state depends only on the current state and action. However, in many real-world systems, external events (e.g., news, disasters, or market shocks) can influence transitions in a non-Markovian way, meaning the future depends on the entire history of such events. This paper addresses the theoretical challenge of learning optimal policies in such settings, providing conditions under which the problem remains tractable and deriving sample complexity guarantees for policy evaluation and improvement.

A new arXiv paper (2404.01897v3) introduces Continuous Spiking Graph Neural Networks (COS-GNN), a unified framework that integrates spiking neural networks (SNNs) with continuous graph neural networks (CGNNs) by embedding SNN-based node representations into an ordinary differential equation (ODE) propagation process. The authors also propose a high-order variant using a second-order ODE to better preserve information, and provide a theoretical proof that COS-GNN mitigates exploding and vanishing gradients, enabling long-range dependency capture. This work addresses a key bottleneck of continuous graph neural networks — their high computational cost, which makes deployment on battery-powered devices difficult — by leveraging the energy efficiency of spiking neural networks. If validated, such hybrid models could enable graph representation learning on edge devices and neuromorphic hardware, broadening the applicability of graph learning beyond data centers. The framework employs SNNs for graph node representation at each time step and integrates them into the ODE process along with time; the high-order variant uses a second-order ODE for spiking representation and continuous propagation to reduce information loss. The paper reports experimental results on graph-based learning tasks showing effectiveness over competitive baselines, though it is a specialized arXiv preprint without peer review or community discussion.

A new arXiv paper (2510.08722v4) presents a controlled empirical study on ImageNet-1K that isolates the effect of semantic positive pairs—different same-class instances—from augmented positive pairs in self-supervised learning. By constructing two matched subsets with identical class composition and training-pair counts, the authors compare representative contrastive and non-contrastive SSL methods under matched conditions, finding that semantic-pair pretraining consistently improves generalization in transfer learning and object detection, with SimCLR showing the largest relative gain. This work clarifies a long-standing ambiguity in self-supervised learning: whether the benefits attributed to semantic positives are real or confounded by augmented positives and false neighbors. The findings offer concrete guidance for designing and selecting SSL frameworks that can effectively exploit semantic pair information, potentially improving representation quality across computer vision tasks. The study uses manually curated semantic-pair datasets and matched augmented-pair baselines from ImageNet-1K, ensuring identical class composition and training-pair counts to isolate the semantic pairing effect. Ablations show that semantic pairs induce invariances beyond the standard transformation pipeline, and among evaluated methods, contrastive learning benefits most strongly, with SimCLR showing the largest relative improvement.

## [CoDRA: Cost-to-Disturbance Ratio for Stable Adversarial RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.12110) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes CoDRA (cost-to-disturbance ratio adversarial training), a framework that expresses the controller-adversary trade-off as a ratio of accumulated cost to accumulated squared disturbance norm, optimized via a self-normalized actor-critic update. Evaluated on two MuJoCo pendulum environments under force and mass sweeps, CoDRA attains the lowest cost at every force level on InvertedDoublePendulum, including a force outside the training range, and in all but one cell of the mass grid. Robust RL policies often degrade in nominal performance when adversarial disturbances are unregulated, and CoDRA removes the need for an explicit disturbance penalty or auxiliary trade-off parameter, potentially making adversarial training more stable and easier to tune. This could benefit researchers and practitioners deploying RL controllers to real systems where sim-to-real gaps and external disturbances are common. CoDRA scales each value term by a stop-gradient normalization constant computed from the current batch, which moderates the adversary's incentive without altering the controller's own update. Its advantage is less pronounced on the milder InvertedPendulum, and the work is a preprint with no community discussion provided, so its broader impact remains unclear.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) policies trained in simulation often degrade when deployed on real systems because the controller must reject external disturbances never seen in simulation. Robust RL addresses this by exposing the controller to perturbations during learning, using techniques such as domain randomization, adversarial minimax formulations, or probabilistic mixtures of protagonist and adversarial behavior. Actor-critic methods combine a policy (actor) with a value function (critic) to optimize decision-making, and adversarial training jointly trains a protagonist and an adversary, but unregulated disturbance mechanisms can destabilize training and collapse nominal performance.

## [Diagnostic-Guided Model Selection for Retinal Atrophy Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16955) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces a diagnostic-guided strategy that empirically decides whether a stochastic or deterministic model class is appropriate for longitudinal medical image prediction, and applies it to fundus autofluorescence (FAF) forecasting. Using a heterogeneous Optos archive of 24,335 images from 9,708 eyes, the diagnostic showed that inter-visit FAF change was weakly tied to elapsed time, leading the authors to build Temporal Retinal U-Net (TRU), a deterministic single-pass predictor that outperformed classical and deep-learning comparators on held-out and zero-shot transfer cohorts. This work challenges the assumption that stochastic generative models are always preferable for longitudinal imaging, showing that added complexity can be unnecessary when disease-related change is small relative to technical variability. It offers medical imaging and machine learning practitioners a practical, task-adaptive framework for choosing model classes, which could improve efficiency and reliability in clinical prediction pipelines. The diagnostic specifically asks whether inter-visit image change is driven by time-dependent disease progression or time-independent acquisition variability, and it found that source-anchored stochastic configurations produced little endpoint-resolvable sample variation. TRU conditions on irregular imaging history and prediction horizon, and while it achieved the strongest overall performance, it showed lower precision in the smaller cross-vendor cohort.

**Background**: Fundus autofluorescence (FAF) imaging is a non-invasive technique that maps lipofuscin distribution in the retinal pigment epithelium and is widely used to diagnose and monitor retinal diseases such as atrophy. Longitudinal modeling aims to predict future images from past ones, but irregular follow-up, device heterogeneity, and acquisition variability complicate the task. Stochastic generative models can capture uncertainty but may add complexity without benefit when predictable disease signal is weak, whereas deterministic models are simpler and always produce the same output for a given input.

<a id="item-225"></a>
## [Rhamba: Region-Aware Hybrid Attention-Mamba Framework for Resting-State fMRI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.01240) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced Rhamba, a region-aware pretraining framework that combines anatomically guided masking with hybrid Attention-Mamba architectures for resting-state fMRI analysis. Pretrained on ABIDE and fine-tuned on COBRE and ADHD-200, the hybrid Mamba-Attention (MA) configuration achieved the highest average AUROC and outperformed state-of-the-art methods. This work shows that hybrid Attention-Mamba designs can improve neuroimaging-based disease classification while remaining interpretable, potentially advancing computer-aided diagnosis of schizophrenia and ADHD. It also provides a flexible template for large-scale fMRI representation learning that balances interpretability, scalability, and performance. Three masking strategies (Any, Majority, Pure) with increasing spatial specificity were tested, and reconstruction loss followed a consistent ordering (Any > Majority > Pure), yet this did not directly translate into downstream performance, which was modest and dataset-dependent. Integrated Gradients was used to identify contributing brain regions, and peak performance depended on the interaction between masking strategy and architecture rather than a single dominant configuration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Resting-state fMRI measures spontaneous brain activity while a subject is not performing a task, and is widely used to study functional connectivity and brain networks. Self-supervised learning pretrains models on unlabeled data by solving synthetic tasks such as masked reconstruction, reducing the need for costly annotations. Mamba is a state space model architecture that offers linear-time sequence modeling as an efficient alternative to Transformers, and hybrid designs interleave it with attention blocks.

<a id="item-226"></a>
## [Git-Assistant Combines LLMs with Automated Planning for Safer Git Operations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09224) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.09224v3) introduces Git-Assistant, an AI assistant that combines Large Language Models with automated planning to help developers execute non-trivial git operations. The system analyzes repository context, translates natural language requests into actionable command sequences, and uses planning techniques to ensure correctness and safety, with experiments showing that planning-augmented variants outperform LLM-only approaches across multiple metrics. Git remains notoriously difficult for many developers, especially when resolving conflicts, rebasing, or recovering from mistakes, so a hybrid AI approach that improves reliability could reduce costly errors in collaborative software development. This work also illustrates a broader trend of augmenting LLMs with formal reasoning to overcome their well-known limitations in structured, multi-step tasks. The evaluation uses synthetic and randomized git environments to compare LLM-only and planning-augmented variants across multiple metrics, with results showing improved reliability and fewer errors when formal reasoning is integrated. However, the abstract is truncated and the paper is an incremental research contribution with no immediate community validation or discussion.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Automated planning is a classic AI problem of finding a sequence of actions that achieves a specified goal, and it has been applied in domains like robotics and logistics. Large Language Models are good at interpreting natural language intent but are known to struggle with formal reasoning and multi-step correctness, which is exactly the gap that planning techniques can help fill. Git is a distributed version control system widely used in software development, but its command-line interface and complex state model make advanced operations error-prone for many practitioners.

<a id="item-227"></a>
## [New Active Learning Method Fixes Flaw in Mismatch-First Audio Labeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13571) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper critiques mismatch-first farthest-traversal (MFFT), a prevailing active learning acquisition strategy for frame-level audio classification, showing it is blind to similarity among selected segments and fails under low annotation budgets. The authors propose mismatch-weighted facility location (MW-FL), which spends the entire budget through a disagreement-weighted coverage objective that penalizes similarity, and it achieves the best area under the learning curve on two multi-label datasets. Sound event detection relies on expensive frame-level strong labels, so improving active learning acquisition directly reduces annotation cost and human effort. The finding that hard disagreement gating is harmful and coverage is the dominant factor offers practical guidance for anyone building annotation-efficient audio systems. MW-FL uses the disagreement signal from MFFT to obtain nonnegative weights for a facility-location objective, applying fixed smoothing without dataset-specific tuning. Experiments across two geometric mechanisms and three ways of using disagreement show that soft disagreement weighting helps on top of coverage, while hard disagreement gating of selection is harmful on both mechanisms.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Active learning selects the most informative unlabeled samples for annotation, reducing labeling cost. In frame-level audio classification, such as sound event detection, strong labels mark the onset and offset of each sound event, making annotation especially expensive. Mismatch-first farthest-traversal (MFFT) combines classifier disagreement with diversity by first picking high-disagreement groups and then spreading the remaining budget via farthest traversal. Facility location is a classic submodular objective that maximizes coverage of selected points while penalizing redundancy.

<a id="item-228"></a>
## [Measuring where ensemble diversity comes from when no answer is uniquely correct](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00285) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.00285v2) measures semantic dispersion across sixteen language models from ten families on a psychotherapeutic case task, finding that the panel produced on average the semantic diversity of 1.69 distinct formulations versus a single-model baseline of 1.43. It introduces a per-model dissent contribution metric — the complement of a model's mean similarity to the other ensemble members — to identify which model is the most divergent voice, and tests as a preregistered hypothesis whether model identity explains a non-zero share of dissent variance across 7,082 formulations from fifteen stratified vignettes. Ensembles are often assumed to supply multiple perspectives, but this work shows that the most divergent voice changes with panel composition, meaning the surfaced outlier describes the ensemble rather than any individual model. This has practical implications for how LLM ensembles are evaluated and deployed in open-ended tasks where correctness is undefined, such as clinical decision support or creative generation. The study uses the Vendi Score — the exponential of the von Neumann entropy of a similarity matrix — as an effective number of distinct elements, but notes that a single aggregate cannot reveal where diversity originates. Model identity was a detectable structuring factor of dissent, yet scale differences pointed in opposite directions across pairs, family grouped models on only five two-member lines, and dissent tracked clinical content rather than the interpretive openness for which the case bank was stratified.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: The Vendi Score is a diversity metric that computes the exponential of the von Neumann entropy of a trace-normalized similarity matrix, giving an effective number of distinct elements in a set. In ensemble learning, diversity is typically treated as a desirable property where contributing models make different errors, but existing metrics validate diversity against a correctness criterion. This paper addresses the gap for tasks where no single answer is uniquely correct, such as psychotherapeutic case formulation, by proposing a per-model dissent contribution derived from the same similarity matrix.

<a id="item-229"></a>
## [TabScope adaptively selects localized vs full-table reasoning for table QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.03395) ⭐️ 6.0/10

TabScope introduces a question-adaptive framework that dynamically chooses between localized sub-table reasoning and full-table reasoning for table question answering. It uses operation-aware table decomposition to build question-specific sub-tables, predicts the question type to pick the reasoning mode, and releases the SLQA benchmark built from real-world long tables. LLM accuracy on table QA often degrades as tables grow, and this work shows the degradation is not uniform across question types, so deciding when to localize matters as much as how. The adaptive approach could improve reliability on large real-world tables and inspire more selective evidence-retrieval strategies in table QA systems. Experiments on WikiTQ and SLQA with GPT-5-mini and LLaMA-3.3-70B show localization is especially effective for lookup and local reasoning questions, while adaptive selection between localized and full-table reasoning gives the best overall performance. The paper also introduces silver reference sub-tables for evaluating evidence selection, though the abstract lacks exact improvement magnitudes and code/datasets are promised only upon publication.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 21, 04:00

**Background**: Table question answering (Table QA) is an NLP task where a model answers natural-language questions using structured tabular data rather than plain text. Large language models perform well on this task, but their accuracy tends to drop as tables get longer because irrelevant cells dilute the context. WikiTQ (WikiTableQuestions) is a widely used benchmark of questions over semi-structured Wikipedia tables, and SLQA is a new benchmark introduced in this paper for real-world long tables.
